from logging.config import fileConfig

from sqlalchemy import engine_from_config
from sqlalchemy import pool
from dotenv import load_dotenv
import os

from alembic import context


load_dotenv()

# this is the Alembic Config object, which provides
# access to the values within the .ini file in use.
config = context.config

# Interpret the config file for Python logging.
# This line sets up loggers basically.
if config.config_file_name is not None:
    fileConfig(config.config_file_name)

# add your model's MetaData object here
# for 'autogenerate' support
from database.session import Base
from database.models import (
    DimVocabulary,DimConcept,FactConceptMap,
    DimLocation, DimTime, DimDisease, 
    DimVector, DimInsecticide, DimIdentificationMethod, 
    DimResistanceTestType, DimSocioeconomicIndicator, DimSource,
    FactEnvironment, FactDiseaseCase, 
    FactVectorObservation, FactBionomics,
    FactDiseaseBurden, FactHealthFacility, 
    FactIntervention, FactInsecticideResistance, 
    FactSocioeconomicCondition,
    )
target_metadata = Base.metadata

# other values from the config, defined by the needs of env.py,
# can be acquired:
# my_important_option = config.get_main_option("my_important_option")
# ... etc.


# for connecting to postgresql database
def get_postgres_url():
    return "postgresql://%s:%s@%s:%s/%s" % (
        os.getenv("DB_USER"),
        os.getenv("DB_PASSWORD"),
        os.getenv("DB_HOST"),
        os.getenv("DB_PORT"),
        os.getenv("DB_NAME"),
    )

# for connecting to sqlalchemy database
def get_sqlite_url():
    return "sqlite:///./%s" % os.getenv("SQL_LITE_DB_NAME", "vbd_cdm.db")

if os.getenv("DB_USER") and os.getenv("DB_HOST"):
    config.set_main_option("sqlalchemy.url", get_postgres_url())
else:
    config.set_main_option("sqlalchemy.url", get_sqlite_url())


def run_migrations_offline() -> None:
    """Run migrations in 'offline' mode.

    This configures the context with just a URL
    and not an Engine, though an Engine is acceptable
    here as well.  By skipping the Engine creation
    we don't even need a DBAPI to be available.

    Calls to context.execute() here emit the given string to the
    script output.

    """
    url = config.get_main_option("sqlalchemy.url")
    context.configure(
        url=url,
        target_metadata=target_metadata,
        literal_binds=True,
        dialect_opts={"paramstyle": "named"},
    )

    with context.begin_transaction():
        context.run_migrations()


def run_migrations_online() -> None:
    """Run migrations in 'online' mode.

    In this scenario we need to create an Engine
    and associate a connection with the context.

    """
    connectable = engine_from_config(
        config.get_section(config.config_ini_section, {}),
        prefix="sqlalchemy.",
        poolclass=pool.NullPool,
    )

    with connectable.connect() as connection:
        context.configure(
            connection=connection, target_metadata=target_metadata
        )

        with context.begin_transaction():
            context.run_migrations()


if context.is_offline_mode():
    run_migrations_offline()
else:
    run_migrations_online()

#-*- coding: utf-8 -*-

import reverse_geocode as rg
import numpy as np
from typing import Tuple, Optional, Dict, List
import pandas as pd
from functools import lru_cache
import logging
from math import radians, sin, cos, sqrt, asin

from database.constants import COUNTRY_LOOKUP

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

# ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 to alpha-3 mapping
ISO_COUNTRY_CODES = {
    'KE': 'KEN', 'GH': 'GHA', 'NG': 'NGA', 'ZA': 'ZAF', 'TZ': 'TZA',
    'UG': 'UGA', 'ET': 'ETH', 'DZ': 'DZA', 'AO': 'AGO', 'BJ': 'BEN',
    'BW': 'BWA', 'BF': 'BFA', 'BI': 'BDI', 'CV': 'CPV', 'CM': 'CMR',
    'CF': 'CAF', 'TD': 'TCD', 'KM': 'COM', 'CG': 'COG', 'CI': 'CIV',
    'DJ': 'DJI', 'EG': 'EGY', 'GQ': 'GNQ', 'ER': 'ERI', 'SZ': 'SWZ',
    'GA': 'GAB', 'GM': 'GMB', 'GN': 'GIN', 'GW': 'GNB', 'LS': 'LSO',
    'LR': 'LBR', 'LY': 'LBY', 'MG': 'MDG', 'MW': 'MWI', 'ML': 'MLI',
    'MR': 'MRT', 'MU': 'MUS', 'MA': 'MAR', 'MZ': 'MOZ', 'NA': 'NAM',
    'NE': 'NER', 'RW': 'RWA', 'ST': 'STP', 'SN': 'SEN', 'SC': 'SYC',
    'SL': 'SLE', 'SO': 'SOM', 'SS': 'SSD', 'SD': 'SDN', 'TG': 'TGO',
    'TN': 'TUN', 'ZM': 'ZMB', 'ZW': 'ZWE'
}

# Country names to ISO codes mapping
COUNTRY_NAME_TO_ISO = {
    'Kenya': 'KE', 'Ghana': 'GH', 'Nigeria': 'NG', 'South Africa': 'ZA',
    'Tanzania': 'TZ', 'Uganda': 'UG', 'Ethiopia': 'ET', 'Algeria': 'DZ',
    'Angola': 'AO', 'Benin': 'BJ', 'Botswana': 'BW', 'Burkina Faso': 'BF',
    'Burundi': 'BI', 'Cabo Verde': 'CV', 'Cameroon': 'CM', 
    'Central African Republic': 'CF', 'Chad': 'TD', 'Comoros': 'KM',
    'Congo': 'CG', "Côte d'Ivoire": 'CI', 'Djibouti': 'DJ', 'Egypt': 'EG',
    'Equatorial Guinea': 'GQ', 'Eritrea': 'ER', 'Eswatini': 'SZ',
    'Gabon': 'GA', 'Gambia': 'GM', 'Guinea': 'GN', 'Guinea-Bissau': 'GW',
    'Lesotho': 'LS', 'Liberia': 'LR', 'Libya': 'LY', 'Madagascar': 'MG',
    'Malawi': 'MW', 'Mali': 'ML', 'Mauritania': 'MR', 'Mauritius': 'MU',
    'Morocco': 'MA', 'Mozambique': 'MZ', 'Namibia': 'NA', 'Niger': 'NE',
    'Rwanda': 'RW', 'São Tomé and Príncipe': 'ST', 'Senegal': 'SN',
    'Seychelles': 'SC', 'Sierra Leone': 'SL', 'Somalia': 'SO',
    'South Sudan': 'SS', 'Sudan': 'SD', 'Togo': 'TG', 'Tunisia': 'TN',
    'Zambia': 'ZM', 'Zimbabwe': 'ZW'
}

# ISO code to country name mapping
ISO_TO_COUNTRY_NAME = {v: k for k, v in COUNTRY_NAME_TO_ISO.items()}


def haversine_distance(lat1: float, lon1: float, lat2: float, lon2: float) -> float:
    """
    Calculate the great circle distance between two points in kilometers.
    
    Args:
        lat1, lon1: First point in decimal degrees
        lat2, lon2: Second point in decimal degrees
        
    Returns:
        Distance in kilometers
    """
    R = 6371  # Earth's radius in kilometers
    
    lat1, lon1, lat2, lon2 = map(radians, [lat1, lon1, lat2, lon2])
    dlat = lat2 - lat1
    dlon = lon2 - lon1
    
    a = sin(dlat/2)**2 + cos(lat1) * cos(lat2) * sin(dlon/2)**2
    c = 2 * asin(sqrt(a))
    
    return R * c


@lru_cache(maxsize=10000)
def get_country_from_coords(lat: float, lon: float) -> Tuple[Optional[str], Optional[str]]:
    """
    Get ISO country name and code from latitude and longitude coordinates.
    
    Uses multiple methods in order of reliability:
    1. reverse_geocode library (requires internet)
    2. Nearest coordinate in COUNTRY_LOOKUP
    3. Bounding box estimation for fallback
    
    Args:
        lat: Latitude in decimal degrees
        lon: Longitude in decimal degrees
        
    Returns:
        Tuple of (country_name, country_code) or (None, None) if not found
    """
    if lat is None or lon is None or pd.isna(lat) or pd.isna(lon):
        return None, None
    
    if not (-90 <= lat <= 90) or not (-180 <= lon <= 180):
        logger.warning(f"Invalid coordinates: ({lat}, {lon})")
        return None, None
    
    # Method 1: Try reverse_geocode first (most accurate)
    try:
        result = rg.search([(lat, lon)])
        if result and len(result) > 0:
            country_name = result[0].get('country')
            country_code_raw = result[0].get('country_code')
            
            # Normalize to ISO alpha-2
            if country_code_raw:
                country_code = country_code_raw.upper()
                # Ensure we have the correct ISO code
                if len(country_code) == 3:
                    # Convert alpha-3 to alpha-2
                    for iso2, iso3 in ISO_COUNTRY_CODES.items():
                        if iso3 == country_code:
                            country_code = iso2
                            break
                elif len(country_code) == 2:
                    # Already alpha-2, just verify
                    pass
                else:
                    country_code = None
            
            if country_code and country_name:
                # Verify the mapping
                if country_code in COUNTRY_NAME_TO_ISO.values():
                    # Get the correct country name for this ISO code
                    correct_name = ISO_TO_COUNTRY_NAME.get(country_code)
                    if correct_name:
                        country_name = correct_name
                return country_name, country_code
                
    except Exception as e:
        logger.debug(f"Reverse geocode failed for ({lat}, {lon}): {e}")
    
    # Method 2: Nearest known coordinate lookup
    country_name, country_code = _get_country_from_nearest(lat, lon)
    if country_code:
        return country_name, country_code
    
    # Method 3: Bounding box fallback for known regions
    country_name, country_code = _get_country_from_bounding_box(lat, lon)
    if country_code:
        return country_name, country_code
    
    logger.warning(f"No country found for coordinates ({lat}, {lon})")
    return None, None


def _get_country_from_nearest(lat: float, lon: float, max_distance_km: float = 200.0) -> Tuple[Optional[str], Optional[str]]:
    """
    Find nearest country using the COUNTRY_LOOKUP table with Haversine distance.
    
    Args:
        lat: Latitude in decimal degrees
        lon: Longitude in decimal degrees
        max_distance_km: Maximum distance in kilometers to consider a match
        
    Returns:
        Tuple of (country_name, country_code) or (None, None) if not found
    """
    if not COUNTRY_LOOKUP:
        return None, None
    
    min_dist = float('inf')
    best_match = None
    
    for (ref_lat, ref_lon), country_info in COUNTRY_LOOKUP.items():
        try:
            dist = haversine_distance(lat, lon, ref_lat, ref_lon)
            if dist < min_dist and dist < max_distance_km:
                min_dist = dist
                best_match = country_info
        except Exception as e:
            logger.debug(f"Error calculating distance for ({lat}, {lon}) to ({ref_lat}, {ref_lon}): {e}")
            continue
    
    if best_match:
        country_name, country_code_raw = best_match
        # Normalize code
        country_code = country_code_raw.upper()
        return country_name, country_code
    
    return None, None


def _get_country_from_bounding_box(lat: float, lon: float) -> Tuple[Optional[str], Optional[str]]:
    """
    Fallback: Use simple bounding box for African countries.
    """
    # African continent bounding box (rough)
    if -35 <= lat <= 37 and -20 <= lon <= 55:
        # More specific checks for large countries
        if 4 <= lat <= 14 and 0 <= lon <= 15:  # West Africa
            return "Nigeria", "NG"
        elif 4 <= lat <= 12 and 34 <= lon <= 42:  # East Africa
            return "Kenya", "KE"
        elif -12 <= lat <= -4 and 28 <= lon <= 36:  # Southern Africa
            return "Zambia", "ZM"
        elif 24 <= lat <= 33 and 25 <= lon <= 35:  # North Africa
            return "Egypt", "EG"
        elif -27 <= lat <= -22 and 15 <= lon <= 30:  # South Africa
            return "South Africa", "ZA"
        elif 8 <= lat <= 12 and -2 <= lon <= 6:  # Ghana
            return "Ghana", "GH"
        elif -6 <= lat <= -1 and 29 <= lon <= 34:  # Tanzania
            return "Tanzania", "TZ"
        elif 0 <= lat <= 4 and 32 <= lon <= 35:  # Uganda
            return "Uganda", "UG"
        elif 4 <= lat <= 15 and 35 <= lon <= 48:  # Ethiopia
            return "Ethiopia", "ET"
        elif 0 <= lat <= 6 and 8 <= lon <= 12:  # Cameroon
            return "Cameroon", "CM"
        elif 24 <= lat <= 37 and -10 <= lon <= -1:  # Morocco
            return "Morocco", "MA"
        elif 11 <= lat <= 20 and -17 <= lon <= -13:  # Senegal
            return "Senegal", "SN"
        elif 4 <= lat <= 10 and 38 <= lon <= 48:  # Somalia
            return "Somalia", "SO"
        elif 8 <= lat <= 22 and 0 <= lon <= 10:  # Togo/Benin/Burkina
            return "Burkina Faso", "BF"
        elif 15 <= lat <= 23 and 1 <= lon <= 11:  # Mali/Niger
            return "Mali", "ML"
    
    return None, None


def get_iso_country_code(country: str) -> Optional[str]:
    """
    Get ISO alpha-2 country code from a country name or code.
    
    Args:
        country: Country name or code (e.g., 'Kenya', 'KE', 'KEN')
        
    Returns:
        ISO alpha-2 code or None if not found
    """
    if not country:
        return None
    
    country = str(country).strip().upper()
    
    # If it's already a valid ISO code
    if country in ISO_COUNTRY_CODES:
        return country
    if country in ISO_COUNTRY_CODES.values():
        # Reverse lookup: code is alpha-3, find alpha-2
        for iso2, iso3 in ISO_COUNTRY_CODES.items():
            if iso3 == country:
                return iso2
    
    # Try to match by name
    country_names = {
        name.upper(): code for name, code in COUNTRY_NAME_TO_ISO.items()
    }
    if country in country_names:
        return country_names[country]
    
    # Try partial match
    for name, code in COUNTRY_NAME_TO_ISO.items():
        if country in name.upper():
            return code
    
    return None


def get_country_name(code: str) -> Optional[str]:
    """
    Get country name from ISO alpha-2 code.
    
    Args:
        code: ISO alpha-2 country code
        
    Returns:
        Country name or None if not found
    """
    if not code:
        return None
    
    code = code.upper()
    return ISO_TO_COUNTRY_NAME.get(code)

# ========================= DIMENSIONS =========================

class DimVocabulary(Base):
    """Registry of external vocabularies used for standardization."""
    __tablename__ = "dim_vocabulary"

    vocabulary_id   = Column(String(100), primary_key=True)   # 'GADM', 'IDOMAL', 'MIRO', 'NCBI'
    vocabulary_name = Column(String(1000), nullable=False)
    source_url      = Column(String(1000))
    version         = Column(String(100))
    description     = Column(String(1000))
    loaded_at       = Column(DateTime, default=datetime.datetime.utcnow)


class DimConcept(Base):
    """Standard concepts extracted from an external vocabulary."""
    __tablename__ = "dim_concept"

    concept_id    = Column(Integer, primary_key=True, autoincrement=True)
    vocabulary_id = Column(String(100), ForeignKey("dim_vocabulary.vocabulary_id"), nullable=False)
    concept_code  = Column(String(100), nullable=False)       # e.g. 'GID_2_KEN.3_1', 'IDOMAL:0000657'
    concept_name  = Column(String(300), nullable=False)       # preferred label
    concept_class = Column(String(100))                         # 'admin2', 'species', 'disease', 'insecticide'
    domain        = Column(String(100))                         # 'location', 'vector', 'disease', 'intervention'
    parent_code   = Column(String(100))                        # hierarchy
    extra         = Column(Text)                               # JSON blob for synonyms, coordinates

    __table_args__ = (
        UniqueConstraint("vocabulary_id", "concept_code", name="uq_concept_vocab_code"),
        Index("idx_concept_vocab_class", "vocabulary_id", "concept_class"),
        Index("idx_concept_domain", "domain"),
    )


class DimTime(Base):
    """Time dimension table"""
    __tablename__ = "dim_time"

    date_id = Column(String(10), primary_key=True)  # YYYY-MM-DD
    date = Column(Date, nullable=False)
    year = Column(Integer, nullable=False)
    month = Column(Integer, nullable=False)
    week = Column(Integer)
    day_of_year = Column(Integer)
    season = Column(String(100))
    is_holiday = Column(Boolean, default=False)


class DimLocation(Base):
    """Location hierarchy (country, region, district, site)"""
    __tablename__ = "dim_location"

    location_id = Column(Integer,  primary_key=True, autoincrement=True)

    location_code = Column(String, unique=True)
    centroid_lat = Column(Float)
    centroid_lon = Column(Float)
    country_code = Column(String(10))
    country_name = Column(String(100))
    admin_level = Column(String(100))
    standard_code = Column(String(100))
    concept_id    = Column(Integer, ForeignKey("dim_concept.concept_id"))
    parent_location_id = Column(
        Integer, ForeignKey("dim_location.location_id"), nullable=True
    )
    origin = Column(String(30), default="source")

    __table_args__ = (
        Index("idx_dim_location_parent", "parent_location_id"),
        Index("idx_dim_location_admin", "admin_level"),
        Index("idx_dim_location_origin", "origin"),
    )

class FactConceptMap(Base):
    """Records how raw CDM values map to standard concepts."""
    __tablename__ = "fact_concept_map"

    map_id         = Column(Integer, primary_key=True, autoincrement=True)
    source_table   = Column(String(100), nullable=False)       # 'dim_location', 'dim_vector', ...
    source_column  = Column(String(100), nullable=False)       # 'name', 'species_name', ...
    source_value   = Column(String(300), nullable=False)      # raw value from ingestion
    vocabulary_id  = Column(String(100), ForeignKey("dim_vocabulary.vocabulary_id"))
    concept_code   = Column(String(100))                       # matched standard code
    match_method   = Column(String(30))                        # 'exact', 'normalized', 'fuzzy', 'manual'
    match_score    = Column(Float)                              # 0-1
    is_approved    = Column(Boolean, default=False)             # human review flag
    mapped_at      = Column(DateTime, default=datetime.datetime.utcnow)

    __table_args__ = (
        UniqueConstraint("source_table", "source_column", "source_value",
                         name="uq_concept_map_source"),
        Index("idx_concept_map_source", "source_table", "source_column"),
        Index("idx_concept_map_approved", "is_approved"),
    )

# ==============================================================
# File: database/schemas.py
# ==============================================================
# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
"""
Pydantic schemas for the CDM.

These mirror the SQLAlchemy models in database/models.py. Each table has a
`*Create` schema (client-facing input, excludes the autoincrement PK) and a
`*Response` schema (full record, includes the PK).
"""
from __future__ import annotations

from datetime import date, datetime
from typing import Optional

from pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict, Field, field_validator


# =============================================================================
# Common validators
# =============================================================================

def _validate_yyyy_mm_dd(v: str) -> str:
    try:
        datetime.strptime(v, "%Y-%m-%d")
    except (TypeError, ValueError):
        raise ValueError("must be a date in YYYY-MM-DD format")
    return v


# =============================================================================
# DIMENSIONS
# =============================================================================

class DimTimeCreate(BaseModel):
    date_id: str = Field(..., description="Date in YYYY-MM-DD format")
    date: date
    year: int
    month: int
    week: Optional[int] = None
    day_of_year: Optional[int] = None
    season: Optional[str] = None
    is_holiday: Optional[bool] = False

    @field_validator("date_id")
    @classmethod
    def _check_date_id(cls, v: str) -> str:
        return _validate_yyyy_mm_dd(v)

    @field_validator("month")
    @classmethod
    def _check_month(cls, v: int) -> int:
        if not 1 <= v <= 12:
            raise ValueError("month must be between 1 and 12")
        return v

    @field_validator("centroid_lon")

class FactVectorObservationCreate(BaseModel):
    location_id: int
    date_id: str
    vector_code: str
    source_code: str
    life_stage: Optional[str] = None
    sex: Optional[str] = None
    collection_method: Optional[str] = None
    count: Optional[int] = None
    density_per_trap: Optional[float] = None
    tested_for_pathogen: Optional[str] = None
    infection_status: Optional[str] = None
    infection_rate: Optional[float] = None

    @field_validator("date_id")
    @classmethod
    def _check_date_id(cls, v: str) -> str:
        return _validate_yyyy_mm_dd(v)

    @field_validator("infection_rate")
    @classmethod
    def _check_rate(cls, v: Optional[float]) -> Optional[float]:
        # Stored as a proportion in [0, 1].
        if v is not None and not (0.0 <= v <= 1.0):
            raise ValueError("infection_rate must be between 0 and 1")
        return v


class FactDiseaseCaseCreate(BaseModel):
    location_id: int
    date_id_onset: str
    date_id_reported: str
    disease_code: str
    identification_method_code: str
    source_code: str
    case_status: Optional[str] = "suspected"
    host_type: Optional[str] = "human"
    age_years: Optional[int] = None
    age_group: Optional[str] = None
    sex: Optional[str] = None
    outcome: Optional[str] = "unknown"
    # Number of cases represented by this row. 1 for individual case records;
    # >1 for aggregated daily surveillance / burden-expanded rows.
    case_count: Optional[int] = Field(1, ge=1)

    @field_validator("date_id_onset", "date_id_reported")
    @classmethod
    def _check_dates(cls, v: str) -> str:
        return _validate_yyyy_mm_dd(v)

    @field_validator("age_years")
    @classmethod
    def _check_age(cls, v: Optional[int]) -> Optional[int]:
        if v is not None and v < 0:
            raise ValueError("age_years cannot be negative")
        return v

    @field_validator("sex")
    @classmethod
    def _check_sex(cls, v: Optional[str]) -> Optional[str]:
        if v is None:
            return v
        v_upper = v.upper()
        if v_upper not in {"M", "F", "OTHER", "UNKNOWN"}:
            raise ValueError("sex must be one of: M, F, OTHER, UNKNOWN")
        return v_upper


class FactInsecticideResistanceCreate(BaseModel):
    """
    Aligned with FactInsecticideResistance in database/models.py.

    A resistance test is a first-class entity, not a child of a vector
    observation. It carries its own location, date, vector code, insecticide,
    test type, and source.
    """
    location_id: int
    date_id: str
    vector_code: str
    insecticide_code: str
    test_type_code: str
    source_code: str

    # Test conditions
    concentration: Optional[float] = None
    exposure_time: Optional[int] = None

    # Raw counts
    n_insect_tested: Optional[int] = None
    n_insect_susceptible: Optional[int] = None
    n_insect_resistant: Optional[int] = None
    n_insect_dead: Optional[int] = None

    # VGSC knockdown-resistance genotype counts
    vgsc_n_insect_tested: Optional[int] = None
    vgsc995l_vgsc995l_n: Optional[int] = None
    vgsc995l_vgsc995f_n: Optional[int] = None
    vgsc995f_vgsc995f_n: Optional[int] = None
    vgsc995l_vgsc995s_n: Optional[int] = None
    vgsc995s_vgsc995s_n: Optional[int] = None
    vgsc995f_vgsc995s_n: Optional[int] = None
    vgsc_generation: Optional[str] = None

    # Outcome
    mortality: Optional[float] = Field(None, description="Mortality percentage (0-100)")
    resistance_status: Optional[str] = None

    @field_validator("date_id")
    @classmethod
    def _check_date_id(cls, v: str) -> str:
        return _validate_yyyy_mm_dd(v)

    @field_validator("mortality")
    @classmethod
    def _check_mortality(cls, v: Optional[float]) -> Optional[float]:
        # Percentage, matching WHO bioassay convention and the
        # consolidation.py / fix_resistance_status.py pipeline.
        if v is not None and not (0.0 <= v <= 100.0):
            raise ValueError("mortality must be a percentage between 0 and 100")
        return v

    @field_validator("n_insect_tested", "n_insect_susceptible",
                     "n_insect_resistant", "n_insect_dead",
                     "vgsc_n_insect_tested")
    @classmethod
    def _check_counts(cls, v: Optional[int]) -> Optional[int]:
        if v is not None and v < 0:
            raise ValueError("insect counts cannot be negative")
        return v

class FactEnvironmentalCreate(BaseModel):
    """
    Aligned with FactEnvironment — long format (location, date, variable, value).

    `variable` is free text but the ETL and analysis pipelines expect the set
    listed in `SUPPORTED_ENV_VARIABLES` below. Passing an unexpected variable
    is allowed but logged by the caller.

    date_id: str

    source_code: str

    @field_validator("date_id")
    @classmethod
    def _check_date_id(cls, v: str) -> str:
        return _validate_yyyy_mm_dd(v)


# Known environmental variable names used by the analysis pipeline.

    "2t", "t2m", "tp", "d2m", "rh", "ndvi", "evi", "lst_day", "lst_night",
    "temperature_mean", "temperature_min", "temperature_max",
    "rainfall_total", "relative_humidity", "aridity_index",
    "potential_breeding_sites",
})

# File: database/session.py
# ==============================================================
#-*- coding: utf-8 -*-

import os
from pathlib import Path
from sqlalchemy import create_engine
from sqlalchemy.orm import sessionmaker, declarative_base
from dotenv import load_dotenv

load_dotenv()

project_root = Path(__file__).parent.parent

def _sqlite_url() -> str:
    name = os.getenv("SQL_LITE_DB_NAME", "vbd_cdm.db")
    path = Path(name)
    if not path.is_absolute():
        path = project_root / path
    return f"sqlite:///{path}"

def _postgres_url() -> str | None:
    user = os.getenv("DB_USER")
    pwd  = os.getenv("DB_PASSWORD")
    host = os.getenv("DB_HOST")
    port = os.getenv("DB_PORT")
    name = os.getenv("DB_NAME")
    if not all([user, pwd, host, port, name]):
        return None
    return f"postgresql://{user}:{pwd}@{host}:{port}/{name}"

DATABASE_URL = os.getenv("DATABASE_URL") or _sqlite_url()

connect_args = {"check_same_thread": False} if DATABASE_URL.startswith("sqlite") else {}

engine = create_engine(
    DATABASE_URL, 
    connect_args=connect_args, 
    echo=False,
    # isolation_level=None,
    )

SessionLocal = sessionmaker(
    autocommit=False, 
    autoflush=False, 
    bind=engine)
Base = declarative_base()

def get_db():
    db = SessionLocal()
    try:
        yield db
    finally:
        db.close()

# File: etl/extractors/file_extractor.py
# ==============================================================
"""
FileExtractor - Reads various file formats into a pandas DataFrame using data_transform_library.
"""

import os
import logging
from typing import Optional, Dict, Any, Union, List

import pandas as pd

# Import the data transformation functions
import sys
sys.path.append(os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(__file__))))
from utilities.data_transform_library import (
    cog_to_df, tif_to_df,
    nc_to_df, grib_to_df, tif_to_df_chunked, timeseries_to_df, timeseries_to_csv,
    dta_to_df, sas7bdat_to_df, sav_to_df,
    geojson_to_df, gpkg_to_df, shp_to_df,
    json_to_df, tsv_to_df, xlsx_to_df, xml_to_df,
    load_yaml_config 
)

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


class FileExtractor:
    """
    Extracts data from a file into a pandas DataFrame.
    Supports many formats: COG/TIFF, NetCDF, GRIB, Stata, SAS, SPSS, GeoJSON, GPKG, Shapefile,
    JSON, TSV, Excel, XML, and CSV (via pandas).
    """

    def __init__(self, extract_mode: str = 'single', large_df_threshold: int = 10_000_000):
        """
        Args:
            extract_mode: One of:
                - 'single' (default): read only the first variable and first time step (fast, low memory).
                - 'timeseries': read all time steps and all variables (may be huge).
            large_df_threshold: Warn if DataFrame rows exceed this value.
        """
        self.extract_mode = extract_mode
        self.large_df_threshold = large_df_threshold

    def extract(self, file_path: str, **reader_kwargs) -> pd.DataFrame:
        """
        Read a file into a pandas DataFrame.

        Args:
            file_path: Path to the input file.
            **reader_kwargs: Additional arguments passed to the specific reader
                             (e.g., sep for CSV, sheet_name for Excel, variable/time_idx for NetCDF).

        Returns:
            pandas DataFrame.

        Raises:
            ValueError: If file extension is not supported or extract_mode is invalid.
            FileNotFoundError: If file does not exist.
        """
        if not os.path.exists(file_path):
            raise FileNotFoundError(f"File not found: {file_path}")

        ext = os.path.splitext(file_path)[1].lower().lstrip('.')
        logger.info(f"Extracting from {file_path} (extension: .{ext}, mode: {self.extract_mode})")

        # Dispatch based on extension
        if ext in ('cog', 'tif', 'tiff'):
            if self.extract_mode == 'chunked':
                # Return a generator
                window_size      = reader_kwargs.pop('window_size', 512)
                band             = reader_kwargs.pop('band', 1)
                nodata_threshold = reader_kwargs.pop('nodata_threshold', -9999.0)
                return tif_to_df_chunked(
                    file_path,
                    window_size=window_size,
                    band=band,
                    nodata_threshold=nodata_threshold,
                )
            else:
                band = reader_kwargs.pop('band', None)
                df = cog_to_df(file_path, band=band) if ext == 'cog' \
                    else tif_to_df(file_path, band=band)
                
        elif ext in ('nc', 'nc4', 'grib', 'grib2'):
            if self.extract_mode == 'single':
                # Use single‑variable, single‑time reader
                variable = reader_kwargs.pop('variable', None)
                time_idx = reader_kwargs.pop('time_idx', 0)
                if ext in ('grib', 'grib2'):
                    df = grib_to_df(file_path, variable=variable, time_idx=time_idx)
                else:
                    df = nc_to_df(file_path, variable=variable, time_idx=time_idx)
            elif self.extract_mode == 'timeseries':
                # Full timeseries - read all variables and time steps (may be huge)
                variables = reader_kwargs.pop('variables', None)
                df = timeseries_to_df(file_path, variables=variables, warn_large=True)
            else:
                raise ValueError(f"Unsupported extract_mode for NetCDF/GRIB: {self.extract_mode}")

        elif ext in ('dta', 'sas7bdat', 'sav'):
            if ext == 'dta':
                df = dta_to_df(file_path, **reader_kwargs)
            elif ext == 'sas7bdat':
                df = sas7bdat_to_df(file_path, **reader_kwargs)
            else:  # sav
                df = sav_to_df(file_path, **reader_kwargs)

        elif ext in ('geojson', 'gpkg', 'shp'):
            if ext == 'geojson':
                df = geojson_to_df(file_path)
            elif ext == 'gpkg':
                df = gpkg_to_df(file_path)
            else:  # shp
                df = shp_to_df(file_path)

        elif ext == 'json':
            lines = reader_kwargs.pop('lines', True)
            df = json_to_df(file_path, lines=lines, **reader_kwargs)

        elif ext == 'tsv':
            df = tsv_to_df(file_path, **reader_kwargs)

        elif ext in ('xlsx', 'xls'):
            sheet = reader_kwargs.pop('sheet_name', 0)
            df = xlsx_to_df(file_path, sheet_name=sheet, **reader_kwargs)

        elif ext == 'xml':
            record_tag = reader_kwargs.pop('record_tag', 'record')
            df = xml_to_df(file_path, record_tag=record_tag, **reader_kwargs)

        elif ext == 'csv':
            # Use pandas directly (with optional chunking inside if needed)
            chunksize = reader_kwargs.pop('chunksize', None)
            if chunksize:
                reader = pd.read_csv(file_path, chunksize=chunksize, **reader_kwargs)
                chunks = [chunk for chunk in reader]
                df = pd.concat(chunks, ignore_index=True)
            else:
                df = pd.read_csv(file_path, **reader_kwargs)

        else:
            raise ValueError(f"Unsupported file extension: .{ext}")

        # Warn if DataFrame is large
        if len(df) > self.large_df_threshold:
            logger.warning(
                f"Extracted DataFrame has {len(df):,} rows (> {self.large_df_threshold:,}). "
                "Consider using chunked extraction or sampling."
            )

        logger.info(f"Extraction complete: {len(df)} rows, {len(df.columns)} columns")
        return df

"""
DBLoader - Loads DataFrames into the CDM database with upsert support and high-performance COPY for PostgreSQL.
"""

import logging
from typing import Dict, Optional, Any
import pandas as pd
from sqlalchemy import create_engine, text, inspect
from sqlalchemy.orm import Session
from sqlalchemy.exc import SQLAlchemyError
from io import StringIO

from database.session import SessionLocal, engine, DATABASE_URL
from database import models

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Natural-key configuration
#
# Each entry maps a table name to:
#   'columns'   — the columns forming the conflict target (must match a unique
#                 constraint in the DB).
#   'sentinels' — values used to replace NULLs in nullable natural-key columns
#                 before insert. PostgreSQL and SQLite treat NULL as distinct in
#                 unique indexes, so without this the constraint would not bite.
# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
NATURAL_KEY_CONFIG = {
    # ---------- Dimensions ----------
    'dim_location':                  {'columns': ['location_code'], 'sentinels': {}},
    'dim_disease':                   {'columns': ['disease_code'],  'sentinels': {}},
    'dim_vector':                    {'columns': ['vector_code'],   'sentinels': {}},
    'dim_insecticide':               {'columns': ['insecticide_code'], 'sentinels': {}},
    'dim_source':                    {'columns': ['source_code'],   'sentinels': {}},
    'dim_identification_method':     {'columns': ['method_code'],   'sentinels': {}},
    'dim_resistance_test_type':      {'columns': ['test_type_code'], 'sentinels': {}},
    'dim_socioeconomic_indicator':   {'columns': ['indicator_code'], 'sentinels': {}},
    'dim_time':                      {'columns': ['date_id'],       'sentinels': {}},

    # ---------- Facts ----------
    'fact_environment': {
        'columns': ['location_id', 'date_id', 'variable'],
        'sentinels': {},
    },
    'fact_disease_burden': {
        'columns': ['location_id', 'year', 'disease_code', 'pathogen_species'],
        'sentinels': {},
    },
    'fact_socioeconomic_condition': {
        'columns': ['location_id', 'year', 'indicator_code'],
        'sentinels': {},
    },
    'fact_health_facility': {
        'columns': ['facility_code'],
        'sentinels': {},
    },
    'fact_vector_observation': {
        'columns': ['location_id', 'date_id', 'vector_code', 'source_code',
                    'life_stage', 'sex', 'collection_method'],
        'sentinels': {'life_stage': '', 'sex': '', 'collection_method': ''},
    },
    'fact_insecticide_resistance': {
        'columns': ['location_id', 'date_id', 'vector_code', 'insecticide_code',
                    'test_type_code', 'source_code', 'concentration'],
        'sentinels': {'concentration': -1.0},
    },
    'fact_bionomics': {
        'columns': ['location_id', 'date_id', 'vector_code', 'source_code'],
        'sentinels': {},
    },
    'fact_intervention': {
        'columns': ['location_id', 'date_start', 'target_disease_code',
                    'source_code', 'intervention_type'],
        'sentinels': {'intervention_type': ''},
    },
    'fact_disease_case': {
        'columns': ['location_id', 'date_id_onset', 'disease_code',
                    'identification_method_code', 'source_code',
                    'case_status', 'age_group', 'sex'],
        'sentinels': {'case_status': '', 'age_group': '', 'sex': ''},
    },
}


class DBLoader:
    """
    Loads DataFrames (from MappingTransformer) into the appropriate database tables.
    Supports upsert (INSERT ON CONFLICT) for PostgreSQL and SQLite.
    For PostgreSQL, optionally uses COPY for ultra‑fast bulk loading when use_copy=True.
    """

    def __init__(self, batch_size: int = 1000, upsert: bool = True, use_copy: bool = False):
        """
        Args:
            batch_size: Number of rows to insert per batch (used for non‑COPY methods).
            upsert: If True, use ON CONFLICT (PostgreSQL) or INSERT OR REPLACE (SQLite).
                    If False, simple append (may fail on duplicates).
            use_copy: (PostgreSQL only) If True, use COPY with a staging table for bulk upsert.
                      Ignored for other databases.
        """
        self.batch_size = batch_size
        self.upsert = upsert
        self.use_copy = use_copy
        self._table_models = self._build_model_map()
        self._db_type = self._detect_db_type()

    def _build_model_map(self) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        """Map table names (as strings) to SQLAlchemy model classes."""
        model_map = {}
        for name, obj in models.__dict__.items():
            if hasattr(obj, '__tablename__'):
                model_map[obj.__tablename__] = obj
        return model_map

    def _detect_db_type(self) -> str:
        """Return 'postgresql', 'sqlite', or 'mysql' based on DATABASE_URL."""
        if DATABASE_URL.startswith('postgresql'):
            return 'postgresql'
        elif DATABASE_URL.startswith('sqlite'):
            return 'sqlite'
        elif DATABASE_URL.startswith('mysql'):
            return 'mysql'
        else:
            raise ValueError(f"Unsupported database type: {DATABASE_URL}")

    def _get_primary_key_columns(self, table_name: str) -> list:
        """Return list of primary key column names for a given table."""
        inspector = inspect(engine)
        pk = inspector.get_pk_constraint(table_name)
        return pk.get('constrained_columns', [])

    def _get_column_types(self, table_name: str) -> Dict[str, str]:
        """Return a dict mapping column name to SQL type (e.g., 'INTEGER', 'TEXT')."""
        inspector = inspect(engine)
        columns = inspector.get_columns(table_name)
        return {col['name']: str(col['type']) for col in columns}

    def _get_conflict_targets(self, table_name: str, pk_cols: list) -> list:
        """
        Return the natural-key columns that form the ON CONFLICT target for a table.
        Falls back to primary keys only if the table is not in NATURAL_KEY_CONFIG.
        """
        config = NATURAL_KEY_CONFIG.get(table_name)
        if config and config['columns']:
            return list(config['columns'])
        return pk_cols

    def _prepare_for_upsert(self, df: pd.DataFrame, table_name: str) -> pd.DataFrame:
        """
        Apply natural-key hygiene to a DataFrame before insert:
            1. Replace NULLs in nullable natural-key columns with sentinels
            (so the unique constraint catches duplicates reliably).
            2. Drop duplicate rows within the batch, keeping the last occurrence
            (PostgreSQL rejects multi-row ON CONFLICT that touches the same
            target row twice within a single INSERT).
        """
        config = NATURAL_KEY_CONFIG.get(table_name)
        if not config:
            return df

        df = df.copy()

        # 1. Sentinels for nullable natural-key columns.
        for col, sentinel in config['sentinels'].items():
            if col in df.columns:
                df[col] = df[col].where(df[col].notna(), sentinel)

        # 2. Deduplicate within the batch by natural key.
        natural_cols = [c for c in config['columns'] if c in df.columns]
        if natural_cols and len(natural_cols) == len(config['columns']):
            before = len(df)
            df = df.drop_duplicates(subset=natural_cols, keep='last')
            if len(df) < before:
                logger.info(
                    f"  Deduplicated {before - len(df)} rows within batch "
                    f"for {table_name} by natural key"
                )
        elif natural_cols:
            logger.warning(
                f"  {table_name}: natural key columns {config['columns']} are not "
                f"all present in the DataFrame; skipping in-batch deduplication."
            )

        return df

    # -------------------------------------------------------------------------
    # PostgreSQL COPY with staging table upsert (high performance for millions of rows)
    # -------------------------------------------------------------------------
    def _copy_upsert_postgresql(self, df: pd.DataFrame, table_name: str, model: Any):
        """
        Use PostgreSQL COPY to bulk load data into a temporary staging table,
        then merge into the target table with ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE.
        Safe against modifying autoincrement primary keys.

        Only columns with at least one non-null value in the incoming batch
        participate in the DO UPDATE SET clause. A source that does not
        populate a column cannot clobber an existing value at the same
        natural key.
        """
        pk_cols = self._get_primary_key_columns(table_name)
        conflict_targets = self._get_conflict_targets(table_name, pk_cols)

        # Staging table setup
        staging_table = f"{table_name}_staging"
        model_columns = [c.name for c in model.__table__.columns]

        # We align DataFrame columns with model schema
        df = df[[col for col in df.columns if col in model_columns]].copy()
        for col in model_columns:
            if col not in df.columns:
                df[col] = None

        # Exclude autoincrement primary keys if they are entirely Null in the DataFrame
        # This prevents Postgres from overwriting auto-generated IDs with NULL values in staging
        for pk in pk_cols:
            if df[pk].isnull().all():
                df = df.drop(columns=[pk])

        # Convert DataFrame to CSV string in memory for COPY
        csv_buffer = StringIO()
        df.to_csv(csv_buffer, index=False, header=False, na_rep='\\N')
        csv_buffer.seek(0)

        with SessionLocal() as session:
            session.execute(text(f"DROP TABLE IF EXISTS {staging_table}"))
            session.execute(text(f"""
                CREATE TEMP TABLE {staging_table} (LIKE {table_name} INCLUDING DEFAULTS)
            """))
            session.commit()

            # Copy standard columns
            raw_conn = session.connection().connection
            cursor = raw_conn.cursor()
            try:
                cursor.copy_expert(
                    f"COPY {staging_table} ({', '.join(df.columns)}) FROM STDIN WITH (FORMAT CSV, NULL '\\N')",
                    csv_buffer
                )
                raw_conn.commit()
                logger.info(f"COPY loaded {len(df)} rows into staging table {staging_table}")
            except Exception as e:
                raw_conn.rollback()
                logger.error(f"COPY failed: {e}")
                raise
            finally:
                cursor.close()

            # Perform upsert: EXCLUDE primary keys and unique constraint targets
            # from SET updates. Also exclude any column that carries no value at
            # all in this batch — such a source has no opinion about that column
            # and must not overwrite existing data.
            update_cols = [
                col for col in df.columns
                if col not in pk_cols
                and col not in conflict_targets
                and df[col].notna().any()
            ]

            conflict_target_str = ", ".join(conflict_targets)

            if update_cols:
                update_set = ", ".join([f"{col} = EXCLUDED.{col}" for col in update_cols])
                upsert_sql = f"""
                    INSERT INTO {table_name} ({', '.join(df.columns)})
                    SELECT {', '.join(df.columns)} FROM {staging_table}
                    ON CONFLICT ({conflict_target_str}) DO UPDATE SET {update_set}
                """
            else:
                # Fallback to do nothing on conflict if no updating fields exist
                upsert_sql = f"""
                    INSERT INTO {table_name} ({', '.join(df.columns)})
                    SELECT {', '.join(df.columns)} FROM {staging_table}
                    ON CONFLICT ({conflict_target_str}) DO NOTHING
                """
            session.execute(text(upsert_sql))
            session.execute(text(f"DROP TABLE {staging_table}"))
            session.commit()
            logger.info(f"Upserted {len(df)} rows into {table_name} via COPY and staging")
            return len(df)


    # -------------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Standard batched upsert (for smaller datasets or non‑PostgreSQL)
    # -------------------------------------------------------------------------
    def _upsert_postgresql(self, df: pd.DataFrame, table_name: str, model: Any) -> int:
        """
        Batched upsert for PostgreSQL.

        Only columns with at least one non-null value in the incoming batch
        participate in the DO UPDATE SET clause. A source that does not
        populate a column cannot clobber an existing value at the same
        natural key.
        """
        from sqlalchemy.dialects.postgresql import insert

        pk_cols = self._get_primary_key_columns(table_name)
        conflict_targets = self._get_conflict_targets(table_name, pk_cols)

        records = df.to_dict(orient='records')
        for record in records:
            for pk in pk_cols:
                if record.get(pk) is None:
                    record.pop(pk, None)

        total_written = 0
        with SessionLocal() as session:
            for i in range(0, len(records), self.batch_size):
                batch = records[i:i + self.batch_size]
                stmt = insert(model).values(batch)

                update_dict = {
                    col: getattr(stmt.excluded, col)
                    for col in df.columns
                    if col not in pk_cols
                    and col not in conflict_targets
                    and df[col].notna().any()
                }

                if update_dict:
                    upsert_stmt = stmt.on_conflict_do_update(
                        index_elements=conflict_targets, set_=update_dict
                    )
                else:
                    upsert_stmt = stmt.on_conflict_do_nothing(
                        index_elements=conflict_targets
                    )

                result = session.execute(upsert_stmt)
                rc = getattr(result, "rowcount", None)
                if rc is None or rc < 0:
                    rc = len(batch)
                total_written += rc
                session.commit()

                logger.debug(
                    f"Upserted batch {i // self.batch_size + 1} into {table_name}"
                )
        return total_written


    def _upsert_sqlite(self, df: pd.DataFrame, table_name: str, model: Any) -> int:
        """
        Upsert for SQLite using ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE rather than INSERT OR REPLACE
        to prevent deletion/regeneration of autoincremented primary keys.

        Only columns with at least one non-null value in the incoming batch
        participate in the DO UPDATE SET clause. A source that does not
        populate a column cannot clobber an existing value at the same
        natural key. This is the fix that prevents e.g. the MAP Pf Incidence
        Rate source from wiping the cases_mean column that the MAP Pf
        Incidence Count source populated a moment earlier.

        Returns the number of rows actually written (inserted or updated).
        """
        from sqlalchemy.dialects.sqlite import insert

        pk_cols = self._get_primary_key_columns(table_name)
        conflict_targets = self._get_conflict_targets(table_name, pk_cols)

        # Exclude PKs, conflict targets, and any column that is entirely NULL
        # in this batch from the update block.
        update_cols = [
            col for col in df.columns
            if col not in pk_cols
            and col not in conflict_targets
            and df[col].notna().any()
        ]

        records = df.to_dict(orient='records')

        # Drop None-valued PKs so SQLite can autoincrement them.
        for record in records:
            for pk in pk_cols:
                if record.get(pk) is None:
                    record.pop(pk, None)

        total_written = 0

        with SessionLocal() as session:
            for i in range(0, len(records), self.batch_size):
                batch = records[i:i + self.batch_size]
                if not batch:
                    continue

                stmt = insert(model).values(batch)

                if update_cols and conflict_targets:
                    upsert_stmt = stmt.on_conflict_do_update(
                        index_elements=conflict_targets,
                        set_={col: stmt.excluded[col] for col in update_cols},
                    )
                elif conflict_targets:
                    upsert_stmt = stmt.on_conflict_do_nothing(
                        index_elements=conflict_targets,
                    )
                else:
                    # No conflict target defined for this table; plain insert.
                    upsert_stmt = stmt

                result = session.execute(upsert_stmt)

                # sqlite3 returns a proper rowcount for ON CONFLICT; fall back to
                # batch length if the driver reports -1 or None.
                rc = getattr(result, "rowcount", None)
                if rc is None or rc < 0:
                    rc = len(batch)

                session.commit()
                total_written += rc

                logger.debug(
                    f"Upserted batch {i // self.batch_size + 1} "
                    f"({rc} rows) into {table_name}"
                )

        return total_written


    def _simple_insert(self, df: pd.DataFrame, table_name: str) -> int:
        written = 0
        for i in range(0, len(df), self.batch_size):
            chunk = df.iloc[i:i + self.batch_size]
            chunk.to_sql(table_name, engine, if_exists='append', index=False, method='multi')
            written += len(chunk)
            logger.debug(f"Inserted batch {i // self.batch_size + 1} into {table_name}")
        return written

    # -------------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Public methods
    # -------------------------------------------------------------------------
    def load_table(self, table_name: str, df: pd.DataFrame) -> int:
        """
        Load a DataFrame into the given table.

        Returns the number of rows actually written to the database (inserted
        or updated). This can differ from ``len(df)`` because
        ``_prepare_for_upsert`` may collapse duplicate rows within the batch
        before insert.

        Autoincrement primary keys are exempt from the NOT NULL guard below.
        A missing autoincrement PK means "the database will assign one", not
        "this row is malformed". Without the exemption, every table whose PK
        is autoincremented (dim_location, dim_concept, and every fact table)
        arrives here with a column of None values and is filtered down to zero
        rows, aborting the ETL silently for that source.
        """
        if df.empty:
            logger.info(f"No data to load for table {table_name}")
            return 0

        if table_name not in self._table_models:
            raise ValueError(
                f"Unknown table: {table_name}. "
                f"Available: {list(self._table_models.keys())}"
            )

        model = self._table_models[table_name]
        input_rows = len(df)

        inspector = inspect(engine)
        model_columns = [c.name for c in model.__table__.columns]
        model_col_map = {c.name: c for c in model.__table__.columns}

        # Columns the database will populate itself.
        pk_cols = set(
            inspector.get_pk_constraint(table_name).get("constrained_columns", [])
            or []
        )
        autoincrement_pks = {
            name for name in pk_cols
            if name in model_col_map
            and model_col_map[name].autoincrement is True
        }

        # Keep only model columns; add missing columns as NULL.
        df = df[[col for col in df.columns if col in model_columns]].copy()
        for col in model_columns:
            if col not in df.columns:
                df[col] = None

        # ------------------------------------------------------------------
        # NOT NULL column handling
        #
        # Two-stage pass.
        #
        # Stage 1: fill NaN values in NOT NULL columns from the model's
        #          Python-side default, when one exists and is a plain scalar.
        #          This rescues columns like fact_disease_case.case_count,
        #          which is NOT NULL with server_default='1' but which the
        #          mapping transformer leaves as None when the source CSV does
        #          not carry the column.
        #
        # Stage 2: drop rows that still have NaN in a NOT NULL column that
        #          has NO default at all. Columns with a server-only default
        #          are NOT dropped here — the server default cannot rescue an
        #          explicit NULL in the INSERT column list, so silently
        #          dropping those rows would hide a real configuration bug.
        #          Log an error instead.
        #
        # Autoincrement PKs are exempt throughout: their absence means "the
        # database will assign one", not "this row is malformed".
        # ------------------------------------------------------------------
        for col_meta in inspector.get_columns(table_name):
            if col_meta.get("nullable", True):
                continue
            col = col_meta["name"]
            if col in autoincrement_pks or col not in df.columns:
                continue

            model_col = model_col_map.get(col)
            py_default = getattr(model_col, "default", None) if model_col is not None else None
            default_arg = getattr(py_default, "arg", None) if py_default is not None else None

            # Stage 1 — fill from a scalar Python-side default.
            if default_arg is not None and not callable(default_arg):
                nan_mask = df[col].isna()
                n_filled = int(nan_mask.sum())
                if n_filled:
                    df[col] = df[col].where(~nan_mask, default_arg)
                    logger.info(
                        f"  {table_name}.{col}: filled {n_filled} NaN values with "
                        f"Python-side default {default_arg!r}"
                    )

        for col_meta in inspector.get_columns(table_name):
            if col_meta.get("nullable", True):
                continue
            col = col_meta["name"]
            if col in autoincrement_pks or col not in df.columns:
                continue

            # Stage 2 — anything still NaN?
            remaining = int(df[col].isna().sum())
            if remaining == 0:
                continue

            # Server-only default: cannot be relied on because the upsert
            # paths pass an explicit NULL for this column. Do not drop the
            # rows — that hides the configuration bug. Log and continue.
            if col_meta.get("default") is not None:
                logger.error(
                    f"  {table_name}.{col}: {remaining} rows still NaN after "
                    f"default fill, but the column has only a server default. "
                    f"Add a Python-side `default=` to the model so the loader "
                    f"can fill these rows, or fix the mapping CSV to supply the "
                    f"column. The INSERT will fail on NOT NULL otherwise."
                )
                continue

            # No default at all — drop the rows.
            before = len(df)
            df = df[df[col].notna()].copy()
            dropped = before - len(df)
            if dropped:
                logger.warning(
                    f"  {table_name}.{col}: dropped {dropped} rows with NaN "
                    f"(column is NOT NULL, no default)"
                )

        # Convert datetimes to date objects to keep COPY simple.
        for col in df.select_dtypes(include=["datetime64[ns]"]).columns:
            df[col] = df[col].dt.date

        # Sentinel-fill and dedup by natural key before upsert.
        df = self._prepare_for_upsert(df, table_name)
        prepared_rows = len(df)

        if prepared_rows == 0:
            logger.info(
                f"No rows to load for {table_name} after natural-key "
                f"preparation (input had {input_rows} rows)"
            )
            return 0

        try:
            if self.upsert:
                if self._db_type == "postgresql":
                    if self.use_copy and prepared_rows > self.batch_size * 2:
                        written = self._copy_upsert_postgresql(df, table_name, model)
                    else:
                        written = self._upsert_postgresql(df, table_name, model)
                elif self._db_type == "sqlite":
                    written = self._upsert_sqlite(df, table_name, model)
                else:
                    written = self._simple_insert(df, table_name)
            else:
                written = self._simple_insert(df, table_name)

            logger.info(
                f"Loaded {written} rows into {table_name} "
                f"(input had {input_rows}, prepared had {prepared_rows})"
            )
            return written

        except SQLAlchemyError as e:
            logger.error(f"Failed to load table {table_name}: {e}")
            raise


    def load_all(self, table_dfs: Dict[str, pd.DataFrame]) -> Dict[str, int]:
        """
        Load multiple tables (the output of MappingTransformer.transform).

        Args:
            table_dfs: Dictionary mapping table name -> DataFrame.

        Returns:
            Dictionary mapping table name -> number of rows loaded.
        """
        results = {}
        for table_name, df in table_dfs.items():
            results[table_name] = self.load_table(table_name, df)
        return results
