# ==============================================================
# File: etl/transformers/mapping_transformer.py
# ==============================================================
"""
MappingTransformer - Interprets CSV mapping files to transform source DataFrames
into CDM table-specific DataFrames.
"""

from datetime import datetime
import calendar
import pandas as pd
import json
from typing import Dict, List, Any, Optional, Union
from collections import defaultdict
import logging

from database import models
from database.session import SessionLocal 

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


class MappingTransformer:
    """
    Transforms a source DataFrame (from any file format) into multiple CDM table DataFrames
    using a mapping CSV that defines source -> target column mappings.
    """

    def __init__(self, mapping_csv_path: str, large_df_threshold: int = 1_000_000):
        """
        Args:
            mapping_csv_path: Path to the mapping CSV file.
            large_df_threshold: Number of rows above which a warning is logged.
        """
        self.mapping = pd.read_csv(mapping_csv_path, quotechar='"')
        self.large_df_threshold = large_df_threshold
        self._validate_mapping()
        self._group_by_target_table()
        self._dimension_cache = {}

    def _validate_mapping(self):
        required_cols = {'source_column', 'target_table', 'target_column', 'transform', 'required'}
        missing = required_cols - set(self.mapping.columns)
        if missing:
            raise ValueError(f"Mapping CSV missing columns: {missing}")

    def _group_by_target_table(self):
        """Group mapping rows by target table name."""
        self.table_mappings = defaultdict(list)
        for _, row in self.mapping.iterrows():
            self.table_mappings[row['target_table']].append(row.to_dict())

    def _apply_map_transform(
        self, 
        value: Any, 
        params: Optional[Union[str, dict]],
        extra_context: Optional[dict] = None) -> Any:
        # Handle dimension lookups with ref parameter
        ref = params.get('ref') if params else None
        if ref:
            # Parse ref: "dim_location.name" or "dim_location.location_name"
            parts = ref.split('.')
            if len(parts) == 2:
                table_name, field_name = parts[0], parts[1]
                # Get the model class
                model_class = self._get_model_for_table(table_name)
                if model_class:
                    # Handle composite lookups from extra_context
                    lookup_value = self._build_lookup_value(value, params, extra_context)
                    return self._lookup_dimension_id(model_class, field_name, lookup_value)
                else:
                    logger.warning(f"Model not found for table: {table_name}")
                    return value
            else:
                logger.warning(f"Invalid ref format: {ref}. Expected 'table.field'")
                return value
        else:
            mapping = params or {}
            # 'default' is an optional key inside params. When present, it
            # is returned if no mapping entry matches. When absent, the
            # input value passes through unchanged. This preserves the
            # original behaviour for callers like itnuse_data that rely on
            # the input surviving; callers like worldbank_indicator that
            # want a fallback label can opt in explicitly.
            default = mapping.get('default', value)
            lookup = {k: v for k, v in mapping.items() if k != 'default'}
            return lookup.get(str(value), default)
    
    
    def _apply_date_parse_transform(
        self,
        value: Any,
        params: Optional[Union[str, dict]]) -> Any:
        """
        Parse date value and return as 'YYYY-MM-DD' string.
        Handles Timestamp, datetime, and strings (with/without time).
        """
        fmt = params.get('format', '%Y-%m-%d') if params else '%Y-%m-%d'
        try:
            # Already datetime-like → format directly
            if isinstance(value, (pd.Timestamp, datetime)):
                return value.strftime('%Y-%m-%d')
            # Try exact format first, fallback to flexible parser
            try:
                dt = pd.to_datetime(value, format=fmt)
            except (ValueError, TypeError):
                dt = pd.to_datetime(value)   # infers time, etc.
            return dt.strftime('%Y-%m-%d')
        except Exception as e:
            logger.warning(f"Date parse failed for '{value}' with format '{fmt}': {e}")
            return None
                
    
    def _apply_derive_category_transform(self, value, params, extra_context=None) -> Any:
        """
        Derive a categorical label from one or more source columns.

        Rules are evaluated top-to-bottom; the first whose conditions all
        hold wins. If none match, `default` is returned.

        Supported conditions:
            Single-column:
                {"col": "indoor_host_percent", "op": ">=", "value": 80}
            Ratio of two columns, computed as a / (a + b):
                {"cols": ["indoor_biting_total", "outdoor_biting_total"],
                "op": "ratio_gte", "value": 0.8}

        ops: >, >=, <, <=, ==, !=, ratio_gt, ratio_gte, ratio_lt, ratio_lte
        """
        if not params:
            return None
        if not extra_context:
            return params.get("default")

        null_tokens = {"", "NA", "N/A", "NONE", "NULL", "NAN", "NR", "-"}
        extra_nulls = params.get("treat_as_null") or []
        if isinstance(extra_nulls, str):
            extra_nulls = [extra_nulls]
        null_tokens.update(str(x).strip().upper() for x in extra_nulls)

        def _num(v):
            if v is None:
                return None
            if isinstance(v, float) and pd.isna(v):
                return None
            if isinstance(v, str):
                s = v.strip()
                if not s or s.upper() in null_tokens:
                    return None
                try:
                    return float(s)
                except (ValueError, TypeError):
                    return None
            try:
                return float(v)
            except (TypeError, ValueError):
                return None

        def _check(cond) -> bool:
            op = cond["op"]

            # Ratio comparison a / (a + b)
            if "cols" in cond:
                a = _num(extra_context.get(cond["cols"][0]))
                b = _num(extra_context.get(cond["cols"][1]))
                if a is None or b is None or (a + b) <= 0:
                    return False
                ratio = a / (a + b)
                v = float(cond["value"])
                return {
                    "ratio_gt":  ratio >  v,
                    "ratio_gte": ratio >= v,
                    "ratio_lt":  ratio <  v,
                    "ratio_lte": ratio <= v,
                }.get(op, False)

            # Single-column comparison
            x = _num(extra_context.get(cond["col"]))
            if x is None:
                return False
            y = float(cond["value"])
            return {
                ">":  x >  y, ">=": x >= y,
                "<":  x <  y, "<=": x <= y,
                "==": x == y, "!=": x != y,
            }.get(op, False)

        for rule in params.get("rules", []):
            if all(_check(c) for c in rule["when"]):
                return rule["label"]

        return params.get("default")


    def _apply_cast_transform(self, value, params):
        target_type = params.get('type', 'str')
        # Treat common "not reported" tokens as null regardless of target type.
        null_tokens = {'NR', 'NF', 'ND', '-', 'NA', 'N/A', 'NONE', 'NULL', 'NAN'}
        if isinstance(value, str) and value.strip().upper() in null_tokens:
            return None
        try:
            if target_type == 'int':
                return int(float(value))
            elif target_type == 'float':
                return float(value)
            elif target_type == 'str':
                return str(value)
            else:
                return value
        except (ValueError, TypeError):
            logger.warning(f"Cast to {target_type} failed for value '{value}'")
            return None
        
    
    def _apply_split_transform(
        self, 
        value: Any, 
        params: Optional[Union[str, dict]]) -> Any:
        delimiter = params.get('delimiter', ',')
        index = params.get('index', 0)
        parts = str(value).split(delimiter)
        if index < len(parts):
            return parts[index].strip()
        logger.warning(f"Split index {index} out of range for value '{value}'")
        return None
    

    def _apply_unit_convert_transform(
        self,
        value: Any,
        params: Optional[Union[str, dict]],
    ) -> Any:
        """
        Affine unit conversion: out = (value / factor) + offset   (divide=True)
                                out = (value * factor) + offset   (divide=False)

        Params
        ------
        factor : float
            Multiplicative scale. Default 1.0.
        offset : float
            Additive offset applied after the scale. Default 0.0.
        divide : bool
            When True, divide by `factor` instead of multiplying. Useful for
            reciprocal-style conversions where expressing the scale as a
            multiplier would be awkward (e.g. 1 / 0.01). Default False.
        default : Any
            Value returned when the input cannot be parsed. If omitted, the
            function returns None, which the caller's default-value logic
            then handles. Default None.
        clamp_min, clamp_max : float | None
            Optional bounds applied after conversion. Typical use: clamp a
            proportion into [0, 1] after multiplying a percentage by 0.01.
        round_to : int | None
            Optional number of decimal places to round to. Useful for
            converting to integer-valued units (e.g. minutes).
        treat_as_null : list[str]
            Additional strings (beyond the defaults) to treat as null.
            Case-insensitive.

        Returns
        -------
        float | default
            The converted value, or `default` if parsing failed.
        """
        if not params:
            params = {}

        factor    = float(params.get("factor", 1.0))
        offset    = float(params.get("offset", 0.0))
        divide    = bool(params.get("divide", False))
        default   = params.get("default")            # None is a valid value here
        clamp_min = params.get("clamp_min")
        clamp_max = params.get("clamp_max")
        round_to  = params.get("round_to")

        null_tokens = {"", "NA", "N/A", "NONE", "NULL", "NAN"}
        extra_nulls = params.get("treat_as_null") or []
        if isinstance(extra_nulls, str):
            extra_nulls = [extra_nulls]
        null_tokens.update(str(x).strip().upper() for x in extra_nulls)

        # ---- Guard the divide case ----------------------------------------
        if divide and factor == 0:
            logger.warning(
                "unit_convert: divide=True with factor=0 is undefined; "
                "returning default"
            )
            return default

        # ---- Null / sentinel handling -------------------------------------
        if value is None:
            return default

        if isinstance(value, float) and pd.isna(value):
            return default

        if isinstance(value, str):
            s = value.strip()
            if not s or s.upper() in null_tokens:
                return default
            value = s  # keep the trimmed string for float() below

        # ---- Numeric coercion ---------------------------------------------
        try:
            v = float(value)
        except (ValueError, TypeError):
            logger.warning(f"unit_convert: could not parse {value!r}")
            return default

        if pd.isna(v):
            return default

        # ---- Apply the affine transform -----------------------------------
        v = (v / factor) if divide else (v * factor)
        v = v + offset

        # ---- Optional bounds ----------------------------------------------
        if clamp_min is not None:
            v = max(v, float(clamp_min))
        if clamp_max is not None:
            v = min(v, float(clamp_max))

        # ---- Optional rounding --------------------------------------------
        if round_to is not None:
            v = round(v, int(round_to))

        return v


    def _apply_coalesce_transform(
        self,
        value: Any,
        params: Optional[Union[str, dict]],
        extra_context: Optional[dict] = None,
    ) -> Any:
        """
        Return the first non-null value among a list of source columns.

        Useful when a source stores the same logical quantity in two
        mutually-exclusive columns — e.g. IRMapper files 4 and 5, where the
        concentration appears in either `Concentration (%)` (WHO tube) or
        `Concentration (µg)` (CDC bottle), never both in the same row.

        Params
        ------
        columns : list[str]
            Column names to try, in priority order. The first column with a
            usable value wins.
        default : Any
            Value returned when every column is empty or unusable.
            Default None.
        treat_as_null : list[str]
            Extra strings to treat as null (case-insensitive, whitespace
            trimmed). The defaults are already recognised:
            "", "NA", "N/A", "NONE", "NULL", "NAN", "NR".

        Returns
        -------
        Any
            The first usable value, or `default`.

        Notes
        -----
        A value is "usable" if it is not None, not NaN, and — for strings —
        does not reduce to a recognised null token. Numeric zero IS usable;
        the transform deliberately does not treat 0 as missing.
        """
        if not params:
            params = {}

        cols    = params.get("columns", [])
        default = params.get("default")

        if not cols or not isinstance(cols, list):
            logger.warning(
                f"coalesce: 'columns' must be a non-empty list, got {cols!r}"
            )
            return default

        if not extra_context:
            return default

        null_tokens = {"", "NA", "N/A", "NONE", "NULL", "NAN", "NR"}
        extra_nulls = params.get("treat_as_null") or []
        if isinstance(extra_nulls, str):
            extra_nulls = [extra_nulls]
        null_tokens.update(str(x).strip().upper() for x in extra_nulls)

        for col in cols:
            if col not in extra_context:
                continue
            v = extra_context[col]
            if v is None:
                continue
            if isinstance(v, float) and pd.isna(v):
                continue
            if isinstance(v, str) and v.strip().upper() in null_tokens:
                continue
            return v

        return default    
        
    
    def _apply_format_date_transform(
        self,
        value: Any,
        params: Optional[Union[str, dict]],
        extra_context: Optional[dict] = None,
    ) -> Any:
        """
        Build a YYYY-MM-DD date_id string from one to three source columns.

        Because `dim_time.date_id` is a full-date primary key, every fact row
        must carry a complete YYYY-MM-DD string. Sources that only know the
        year (or year + month) are still accommodated: the missing components
        are filled from configurable defaults, and whatever precision was
        available is preserved in the returned date.

        Params
        ------
        columns : list[str]
            One to three column names, ordered [year, month, day]. Only the
            first entry (year) is required. Missing trailing entries trigger
            the defaults below.
        default : str | None
            Fallback date_id string, returned when the year is missing or
            out of range. Typically "1970-01-01" or "1900-01-01".
        month_default : int
            Month to use when the month column is absent or unparseable.
            Default 1 (January).
        day_default : int
            Day to use when the day column is absent or unparseable.
            Default 1.
        min_year, max_year : int
            Bounds on the accepted year. Values outside the range fall back
            to `default`. Defaults 1800 and 2200.
        clamp_invalid_day : bool
            When True (the default), a day that is out of range for the
            given month is clamped to the last valid day of that month
            (e.g. Feb 30 -> Feb 28/29). When False, the row falls back to
            `default` instead.

        Returns
        -------
        str | None
            A YYYY-MM-DD date_id string, or `default` if the year could not
            be resolved.
        """
        if not params:
            params = {}

        cols = params.get("columns", [])
        if not isinstance(cols, list) or not (1 <= len(cols) <= 3):
            logger.warning(
                f"format_date: 'columns' must be a list of 1-3 names, got {cols!r}"
            )
            return params.get("default")

        default          = params.get("default")
        month_default    = int(params.get("month_default", 1))
        day_default      = int(params.get("day_default", 1))
        min_year         = int(params.get("min_year", 1800))
        max_year         = int(params.get("max_year", 2200))
        clamp_invalid    = bool(params.get("clamp_invalid_day", True))

        # ---- Internal: coerce a raw cell to a positive integer, or None ----
        def _as_int(raw):
            if raw is None:
                return None
            if isinstance(raw, float) and pd.isna(raw):
                return None
            if isinstance(raw, str):
                s = raw.strip()
                if not s or s.upper() in ("NA", "N/A", "NONE", "NULL", "NAN"):
                    return None
                try:
                    return int(float(s))
                except (ValueError, TypeError):
                    return None
            try:
                return int(float(raw))
            except (ValueError, TypeError):
                return None

        # ---- Year (mandatory) ---------------------------------------------
        raw_year = extra_context.get(cols[0]) if extra_context else None
        year = _as_int(raw_year)
        if year is None or not (min_year <= year <= max_year):
            logger.debug(
                f"format_date: unusable year {raw_year!r}; using default {default!r}"
            )
            return default

        # ---- Month (optional) ---------------------------------------------
        month = month_default
        if len(cols) >= 2:
            raw_month = extra_context.get(cols[1]) if extra_context else None
            m = _as_int(raw_month)
            if m is not None and 1 <= m <= 12:
                month = m
            elif m is not None:
                logger.debug(
                    f"format_date: month {m} out of range; using {month_default}"
                )

        # ---- Day (optional) -----------------------------------------------
        day = day_default
        if len(cols) >= 3:
            raw_day = extra_context.get(cols[2]) if extra_context else None
            d = _as_int(raw_day)
            if d is not None and 1 <= d <= 31:
                day = d
            elif d is not None:
                logger.debug(
                    f"format_date: day {d} out of range; using {day_default}"
                )

        # ---- Assemble and validate ----------------------------------------
        # datetime() raises ValueError on impossible dates (Feb 30, Apr 31, ...).
        try:
            return datetime(year, month, day).strftime("%Y-%m-%d")
        except ValueError:
            if not clamp_invalid:
                logger.debug(
                    f"format_date: invalid date {year}-{month:02d}-{day:02d}; "
                    f"using default {default!r}"
                )
                return default
            # Clamp the day to the last valid day of the month.
            last_day = calendar.monthrange(year, month)[1]
            clamped = datetime(year, month, last_day)
            logger.debug(
                f"format_date: {year}-{month:02d}-{day:02d} invalid; "
                f"clamped to {clamped.strftime('%Y-%m-%d')}"
            )
            return clamped.strftime("%Y-%m-%d")


    def _apply_transform(
        self,
        value: Any,
        transform: str,
        params: Optional[Union[str, dict]],
        extra_context: Optional[dict] = None) -> Any:
        """
        Apply a single transformation to a value.

        Args:
            value: The input value (may be None or NaN).
            transform: Transformation type ('none', 'map', 'date_parse', 'cast', 'split', 'unit_convert', 'format_date').
            params: Optional parameters (dict or, for cast, a string type name).
            extra_context: Additional context for lookups (e.g., other columns from the row)
        Returns:
            Transformed value, or None if transformation fails.
        """
        # Normalise params: if a string is given for cast, treat as {'type': params}
        if isinstance(params, str):
            if params.strip() == '':
                params = {}
            elif transform == 'cast':
                params = {'type': params}
            else:
                params = {}
        elif params is None:
            params = {}
        # For transforms that don't require a raw value, skip the None check.
        if transform not in ('format_date', 'map', 'concat', 'coalesce', 'derive_category'):
            if pd.isna(value) or value is None:
                return None
        if transform == 'none':
            return value
        elif transform == 'map':
            return self._apply_map_transform(value, params, extra_context)
        elif transform == 'date_parse':
            return self._apply_date_parse_transform(value, params)
        elif transform == 'cast':
            return self._apply_cast_transform(value, params)
        elif transform == 'split':
            return self._apply_split_transform(value, params)
        elif transform == 'unit_convert':
            return self._apply_unit_convert_transform(value, params)
        elif transform == 'format_date':
            return self._apply_format_date_transform(value, params, extra_context)
        elif transform == 'coalesce':
            return self._apply_coalesce_transform(value, params, extra_context)
        elif transform == 'derive_category':
            return self._apply_derive_category_transform(value, params, extra_context)
        else:
            logger.warning(f"Unknown transform type: '{transform}'")
            return value
        
    def _get_model_for_table(self, table_name: str):
        """Get the SQLAlchemy model class for a given table name."""
        # Map table names to model classes
        model_map = {
            'dim_vocabulary': models.DimVocabulary,
            'dim_concept': models.DimConcept,
            'dim_location': models.DimLocation,
            'dim_time': models.DimTime,
            'dim_disease': models.DimDisease,
            'dim_vector': models.DimVector,
            'dim_insecticide': models.DimInsecticide,
            'dim_identification_method': models.DimIdentificationMethod,
            'dim_resistance_test_type': models.DimResistanceTestType,
            'dim_socioeconomic_indicator': models.DimSocioeconomicIndicator,
            'dim_source': models.DimSource,
            'fact_concept_map': models.FactConceptMap,
            'fact_vector_observation': models.FactVectorObservation,
            'fact_disease_case': models.FactDiseaseCase,
            'fact_insecticide_resistance': models.FactInsecticideResistance,
            'fact_bionomics': models.FactBionomics,
            'fact_intervention': models.FactIntervention,
            'fact_socioeconomic_condition': models.FactSocioeconomicCondition,
            'fact_health_facility': models.FactHealthFacility,
            'fact_disease_burden': models.FactDiseaseBurden,
            'fact_environment': models.FactEnvironment,
        }
        return model_map.get(table_name)
    
    def _build_lookup_value(self, original_value: Any, params: dict, extra_context: Optional[dict]) -> Any:
        """
        Build the lookup value using the source column value and optionally other columns.
        
        Args:
            original_value: The value from the source column
            params: Transform parameters (may contain 'columns' for composite lookups)
            extra_context: Additional row data for composite lookups
        
        Returns:
            The value to use for the lookup
        """
        # Check if we need to combine multiple columns for uniqueness
        columns = params.get('columns')
        if columns and extra_context:
            # Combine multiple columns
            parts = []
            for col in columns:
                if col in extra_context and pd.notna(extra_context[col]):
                    parts.append(str(extra_context[col]))
            if parts:
                # Use separator or default to '/'
                separator = params.get('separator', '/')
                return separator.join(parts)
        
        # Single column lookup
        return original_value
    
    def _lookup_dimension_id(self, model_class, field_name: str, lookup_value: Any) -> Optional[int]:
        """
        Look up the primary key ID from a dimension table.
        
        Args:
            model_class: SQLAlchemy model class
            field_name: The field to match (e.g., 'name', 'code')
            lookup_value: The value to search for
        
        Returns:
            The ID (primary key) of the matching record, or None if not found
        """
        if lookup_value is None or (isinstance(lookup_value, float) and pd.isna(lookup_value)):
            return None
        # Check cache first
        cache_key = f"{model_class.__tablename__}_{field_name}_{lookup_value}"
        if cache_key in self._dimension_cache:
            return self._dimension_cache[cache_key]
        try:
            with SessionLocal() as session:
                # Build query dynamically
                query = session.query(model_class)
                # Handle different field types (string vs numeric)
                # if isinstance(lookup_value, str):
                #     query = query.filter(getattr(model_class, field_name).ilike(lookup_value))
                # else:
                #     query = query.filter(getattr(model_class, field_name) == lookup_value)
                query = query.filter(getattr(model_class, field_name) == lookup_value)
                result = query.first()
                if result:
                    # Get the primary key
                    pk_column = model_class.__table__.primary_key.columns.values()[0]
                    pk_value = getattr(result, pk_column.name)
                    self._dimension_cache[cache_key] = pk_value
                    logger.info(f"Looked up for PK Column {pk_column} with {field_name} == {lookup_value} in model {model_class}, Result ID: {pk_value}")
                    return pk_value
                else:
                    logger.warning(f"No match found for {model_class.__tablename__}.{field_name} = '{lookup_value}'")
                    return None
        except Exception as e:
            logger.error(f"Error during dimension lookup: {e}")
            return None
        

    def _determine_raw_value(self, source_row, target_table, mapping) -> Any:
        value = None

        # 1. Static value is the value itself; no source column needed.
        if pd.notna(mapping.get('static_value')):
            return mapping['static_value']

        # 2. Empty source_column → defer to the transform.
        src_col = mapping.get('source_column')
        if src_col is None or (isinstance(src_col, float) and pd.isna(src_col)):
            return None

        # 3. Otherwise read from the source row.
        if src_col not in source_row:
            if mapping.get('required', False):
                raise ValueError(
                    f"Required source column '{src_col}' missing in data (table {target_table})"
                )
            value = mapping.get('default_value')
        else:
            value = source_row[src_col]
            if pd.notna(mapping.get('source_data_type')):
                dtype = mapping['source_data_type']
                try:
                    if dtype == 'int':
                        value = int(float(value)) if pd.notna(value) else None
                    elif dtype == 'float':
                        value = float(value) if pd.notna(value) else None
                    elif dtype == 'str':
                        value = str(value) if pd.notna(value) else None
                except (ValueError, TypeError):
                    value = None
        return value
    

    def _apply_transformation(self, idx, raw_value, source_row, mapping, row_context):
        value = raw_value
        transform = mapping['transform']
        params = None
        if pd.notna(mapping.get('transform_params')):
            params_str = mapping['transform_params']
            # Skip if the string is empty or only whitespace
            if params_str and params_str.strip():
                try:
                    params = json.loads(params_str)
                except json.JSONDecodeError as e:
                    logger.warning(f"Invalid JSON in transform_params: '{params_str}' - {e}")
                    params = {}
            else:
                params = {}
        if transform == 'concat':
            cols = params.get('columns', []) if params else []
            sep = params.get('separator', ' ') if params else ' '
            parts = []
            for col in cols:
                if col in row_context and pd.notna(row_context[col]):
                    parts.append(str(row_context[col]))
                else:
                    logger.warning(f"Concat: column '{col}' missing or null in row {idx}")
            value = sep.join(parts) if parts else mapping.get('default_value')
        else:
            value = self._apply_transform(raw_value, transform, params, extra_context=row_context)
        return value

    def _apply_default_transform(self, value, mapping, target_table):
        if value is None or (isinstance(value, float) and pd.isna(value)):
            if mapping.get('required', False):
                raise ValueError(
                    f"Required field '{mapping['target_column']}' in table '{target_table}' has null value"
                )
            value = mapping.get('default_value')
        return value

    def _build_table_df(self, source_df: pd.DataFrame, target_table: str) -> pd.DataFrame:
        rows = []
        mappings = self.table_mappings[target_table]
        records = source_df.to_dict(orient='records')      # once, not per row

        for idx, row_context in enumerate(records):
            record = {}
            for mapping in mappings:
                value = self._determine_raw_value(row_context, target_table, mapping)
                value = self._apply_transformation(idx, value, row_context, mapping, row_context)
                value = self._apply_default_transform(value, mapping, target_table)
                record[mapping['target_column']] = value
            rows.append(record)
        return pd.DataFrame(rows)


    def transform(self, source_df: pd.DataFrame,
              target_filter: Optional[set] = None) -> Dict[str, pd.DataFrame]:
        """
        Transform source DataFrame into a dictionary of table -> DataFrame.

        Args:
            source_df:      Source DataFrame.
            target_filter:  If provided, only build DataFrames for these target
                            tables. Used to keep dimension-only and fact-only
                            transform passes separated.
        """
        if len(source_df) > self.large_df_threshold:
            logger.warning(
                f"Source DataFrame has {len(source_df):,} rows (> {self.large_df_threshold:,}). "
                "Row-wise transformation may be slow. Consider chunking the source data."
            )
        result = {}
        for target_table in self.table_mappings.keys():
            if target_filter is not None and target_table not in target_filter:
                continue
            df = self._build_table_df(source_df, target_table)
            df = df.dropna(how='all')
            if not df.empty:
                result[target_table] = df
                logger.info(f"Built {len(df)} rows for table '{target_table}'")
        return result

# ==============================================================
# File: main.py

# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-

"""
CDM Integration Framework - Orchestrator
Reads base_config.yaml, runs ETL pipelines for all data sources.
"""

import os
import sys
import logging
import argparse
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Dict, Any, List, Optional, Union
from datetime import datetime
from sqlalchemy import text

import pandas as pd
from dotenv import load_dotenv


from utilities.consolidation import (
    DataConsolidator, run_consolidation,
    enrich_location_dataframe,
    ISO_COUNTRY_CODES,
    get_iso_country_code,
)

# Add project root to path
PROJECT_ROOT = Path(__file__).parent.absolute()
sys.path.insert(0, str(PROJECT_ROOT))

# Local imports
from database import models
from database.session import SessionLocal
from utilities.data_transform_library import load_yaml_config
from etl.extractors.file_extractor import FileExtractor
from etl.transformers.mapping_transformer import MappingTransformer
from etl.loaders.db_loader import DBLoader
from utilities.database_summary import (
    schema_summary_to_csv, 
    export_all_table_samples)

# Setup logging
if not os.path.exists(os.path.join(PROJECT_ROOT, 'logs')):
    os.makedirs(os.path.join(PROJECT_ROOT, 'logs'))
    print(f"Folder '{os.path.join(PROJECT_ROOT, 'logs')}' created.")
else:
    print(f"Folder '{os.path.join(PROJECT_ROOT, 'logs')}' already exists.")

logging.basicConfig(
    level=logging.INFO,
    format='%(asctime)s - %(name)s - %(levelname)s - %(message)s',
    handlers=[
        logging.StreamHandler(sys.stdout),
        logging.FileHandler(PROJECT_ROOT / 'logs' / 'etl.log', mode='a')
    ]
)
logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


def setup_environment():
    """Load environment variables and create necessary directories."""
    load_dotenv()
    (PROJECT_ROOT / 'logs').mkdir(exist_ok=True)
    (PROJECT_ROOT / 'outputs').mkdir(exist_ok=True)
    # Seed the time dimension once, not per source.
    init_dim_time(start_date="1900-01-01", end_date="2026-12-31")


def get_all_files_from_folder(folder_path: str, extension: str) -> List[str]:
    """
    Recursively find all files with a given extension in a folder.

    Args:
        folder_path: Root folder to scan.
        extension: File extension without dot (e.g., 'csv', 'grib').

    Returns:
        List of full file paths.
    """
    ext = extension.lower()
    files = []
    for root, _, filenames in os.walk(folder_path):
        for f in filenames:
            if f.lower().endswith(f'.{ext}'):
                files.append(os.path.join(root, f))
    return files


def get_file_paths(source_config: Dict[str, Any]) -> List[str]:
    """Get list of file paths from source configuration."""
    source_type = source_config.get('type', 'file')
    extension = source_config.get('extension', '').lower()
    source_path = source_config.get('path')
    if not extension:
        raise ValueError(f"Source missing 'extension' field")
    if not source_path:
        raise ValueError(f"Source missing 'path' field")
    if not os.path.isabs(source_path):
        source_path = str(PROJECT_ROOT / source_path)
    if source_type == 'folder':
        file_paths = get_all_files_from_folder(source_path, extension)
        if not file_paths:
            logger.warning(f"No .{extension} files found in folder {source_path}")
        else:
            logger.info(f"Found {len(file_paths)} files in folder {source_path}")
    else:
        file_paths = [source_path]
    return file_paths


def get_mapping_path(source_config: Dict[str, Any]) -> str:
    """Get and validate mapping CSV path."""
    mapping_path = source_config.get('matching')
    if not mapping_path:
        raise ValueError(f"Source missing 'matching' field")
    if not os.path.isabs(mapping_path):
        mapping_path = str(PROJECT_ROOT / mapping_path)
    return mapping_path


def init_dim_time(start_date: str, end_date: str):
    from datetime import datetime, timedelta
    logger.info(f"Initializing dim_time from {start_date} to {end_date}")
    start = datetime.strptime(start_date, '%Y-%m-%d')
    end   = datetime.strptime(end_date, '%Y-%m-%d')
    if start > end:
        raise ValueError(f"start_date ({start_date}) must be before end_date ({end_date})")

    with SessionLocal() as session:
        existing_ids = {
            row[0] for row in session.execute(text("SELECT date_id FROM dim_time"))
        }
        to_insert = []
        current = start
        while current <= end:
            date_id = current.strftime('%Y-%m-%d')
            if date_id not in existing_ids:
                to_insert.append(models.DimTime(
                    date_id=date_id,
                    date=current.date(),
                    year=current.year,
                    month=current.month,
                    week=current.isocalendar()[1],
                    day_of_year=current.timetuple().tm_yday,
                    season='dry' if current.month in (11, 12, 1, 2, 3) else 'wet',
                    is_holiday=False,
                ))
            current += timedelta(days=1)

        if to_insert:
            session.bulk_save_objects(to_insert)
            session.commit()
        logger.info(f"  Created {len(to_insert)} new time records, "
                    f"{len(existing_ids)} already existed")
                    

def get_dimension_tables() -> List[str]:
    """Return list of dimension tables in order of dependency."""
    return [
        'dim_source',
        'dim_vector',
        'dim_location',
        'dim_disease',
        'dim_insecticide',
        'dim_identification_method',
        'dim_resistance_test_type',
        'dim_socioeconomic_indicator'
    ]


def transform_dimensions(df: pd.DataFrame, mapping_path: str) -> Dict[str, pd.DataFrame]:
    transformer = MappingTransformer(mapping_path)
    dim_tables = set(get_dimension_tables())
    all_tables  = transformer.transform(df, target_filter=dim_tables)
    dim_dfs = {t: all_tables[t] for t in get_dimension_tables()
               if t in all_tables and not all_tables[t].empty}
    return dim_dfs


def load_dimensions(dim_dfs: Dict[str, pd.DataFrame], loader: DBLoader) -> int:
    """Load dimension tables into database. Returns total rows loaded."""
    total_rows = 0
    for table_name in get_dimension_tables():
        if table_name not in dim_dfs:
            continue
        dim_df = dim_dfs[table_name]
        logger.info(f"  Loading dimension {table_name} with {len(dim_df)} rows...")
        if table_name == 'dim_location':
            # Fill country_code from lat/lon for rows that miss it
            dim_df = enrich_location_dataframe(dim_df)
            # Optionally drop rows without location_id (if required)
            if 'location_id' in dim_df.columns:
                before = len(dim_df)
                dim_df = dim_df.dropna(subset=['location_id'])
                if len(dim_df) < before:
                    logger.warning(f"  Removed {before - len(dim_df)} rows with NULL location_id")
        rows = loader.load_table(table_name, dim_df)
        total_rows += rows
        logger.info(f"  Loaded {rows} rows into {table_name}")
    return total_rows


def get_fact_tables() -> List[str]:
    """Return list of fact tables."""
    return [
        'fact_vector_observation',
        'fact_disease_case',
        'fact_insecticide_resistance',
        'fact_bionomics',
        'fact_intervention',
        'fact_socioeconomic_condition',
        'fact_health_facility',
        'fact_disease_burden',
        'fact_environment'
    ]


def transform_facts(df: pd.DataFrame, mapping_path: str) -> Dict[str, pd.DataFrame]:
    transformer = MappingTransformer(mapping_path)
    fact_tables = set(get_fact_tables())
    all_tables  = transformer.transform(df, target_filter=fact_tables)
    fact_dfs = {t: all_tables[t] for t in get_fact_tables()
                if t in all_tables and not all_tables[t].empty}
    return fact_dfs


def load_facts(fact_dfs: Dict[str, pd.DataFrame], loader: DBLoader) -> int:
    total_rows = 0
    for table_name in get_fact_tables():
        if table_name not in fact_dfs:
            continue
        fact_df = fact_dfs[table_name]
        logger.info(f"  Processing fact {table_name} with {len(fact_df)} rows...")
        # Drop rows with null value for fact_environment
        if table_name == 'fact_environment':
            if 'value' in fact_df.columns:
                before = len(fact_df)
                fact_df = fact_df.dropna(subset=['value'])
                logger.info(f"    Dropped {before - len(fact_df)} rows with null value")
            if 'location_id' in fact_df.columns:
                before = len(fact_df)
                fact_df = fact_df.dropna(subset=['location_id'])
                logger.info(f"    Dropped {before - len(fact_df)} rows with null location_id")
        if not fact_df.empty:
            rows = loader.load_table(table_name, fact_df)
            total_rows += rows
            logger.info(f"  Loaded {rows} rows into {table_name}")
        else:
            logger.warning(f"No valid rows to load for {table_name}")
    return total_rows


def run_ingestion_for_source(source_config: Dict[str, Any]) -> bool:
    """
    Run the complete ETL pipeline for a single data source.
    """
    import re
    source_name = source_config.get('source_name', 'unknown')
    logger.info(f"===== Processing source: {source_name} =====")
    try:
        # Step 1: Get file paths
        file_paths = get_file_paths(source_config)
        if not file_paths:
            logger.warning(f"No files to process for {source_name}")
            return False
        # Step 2: Get mapping path
        mapping_path = get_mapping_path(source_config)
        
        # Step 3: Create loader
        loader = DBLoader(batch_size=10000, upsert=True, use_copy=True)
        
        # Step 4: Process each file
        total_dim_rows_loaded = 0
        total_fact_rows_loaded = 0
        extract_mode = source_config.get('extract_mode', 'single')
        reader_args = source_config.get('extension_options', {})
        
        for file_path in file_paths:
            logger.info(f"Processing file: {file_path}")
            # Extract year from filename (if present)
            # year_match = re.search(r'(\d{4})(?=\.[^.]+$)', os.path.basename(file_path))
            # year_match = re.search(r'(\d{4})(?=[^.]*$)', os.path.basename(file_path))
            # year = int(year_match.group(1)) if year_match else None
            stem = os.path.splitext(os.path.basename(file_path))[0]
            year_candidates = re.findall(r'(\d{4})', stem)
            year = int(year_candidates[-1]) if year_candidates else None
            
            # Step 4a: Extract data
            extractor = FileExtractor(extract_mode=extract_mode, large_df_threshold=10_000_000)
            result = extractor.extract(file_path, **reader_args)

            if extract_mode == 'chunked':
                chunk_iter = result
                chunk_count = 0
                for chunk_df in chunk_iter:
                    chunk_count += 1
                    if chunk_count % 10 == 0:
                        logger.info(f"Processed {chunk_count} chunks so far...")
                    if chunk_df.empty:
                        continue
                    if year is not None:
                        chunk_df['year'] = year

                    # Load dimensions on EVERY chunk. Upsert makes this idempotent, and
                    # each chunk may contain new pixels that later chunks' facts point at.
                    dim_dfs = transform_dimensions(chunk_df, mapping_path)
                    if dim_dfs:
                        total_dim_rows_loaded += load_dimensions(dim_dfs, loader)

                    # Facts.
                    fact_dfs = transform_facts(chunk_df, mapping_path)
                    if fact_dfs:
                        total_fact_rows_loaded += load_facts(fact_dfs, loader)
            else:
                # Normal single DataFrame
                df = result
                if df.empty:
                    continue
                if year is not None:
                    df['year'] = year
                # Transform and load dimensions
                dim_dfs = transform_dimensions(df, mapping_path)
                if dim_dfs:
                    total_dim_rows_loaded += load_dimensions(dim_dfs, loader)
                # Transform and load facts
                fact_dfs = transform_facts(df, mapping_path)
                if fact_dfs:
                    total_fact_rows_loaded += load_facts(fact_dfs, loader)
        
        logger.info(f"Source '{source_name}' completed. Total dim rows loaded: {total_dim_rows_loaded}. "
                     f"Total facts rows loaded: {total_fact_rows_loaded}")
        return True
    except Exception as e:
        logger.error(f"Failed to process source '{source_name}': {e}", exc_info=True)
        return False


def run_consolidation_step(core_cfg: Dict[str, Any]) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """
    Run the full data consolidation step.

    Parameters
    ----------
    core_cfg : dict
        The `core:` block from base_config.yaml. Expected keys:
            countries         : list[str]  — ISO-2 or ISO-3 codes, e.g. ['KEN']
            vocabulary.gadm_gpkg : str     — path to the GADM GeoPackage

    Returns
    -------
    dict
        The result dictionary from DataConsolidator.consolidate_all(),
        or {'error': <message>} if consolidation failed.
    """
    logger.info("=" * 60)
    logger.info("RUNNING DATA CONSOLIDATION")
    logger.info("=" * 60)

    core_cfg = core_cfg or {}

    vocabulary    = core_cfg.get("vocabulary", {}) or {}
    gadm_gpkg     = vocabulary.get("gadm_gpkg")
    country_codes = core_cfg.get("countries")

    # Normalise country codes: GADM layer filtering expects ISO-3
    # (GID_0 values like 'KEN'). Accept either ISO-2 or ISO-3 from config
    # and translate to ISO-3 here so the caller does not have to know.
    if country_codes:
        iso3_codes = []
        for code in country_codes:
            iso2 = get_iso_country_code(code)
            if iso2 is None:
                logger.warning(
                    f"Unrecognised country code '{code}' in core.countries; "
                    f"passing through unchanged."
                )
                iso3_codes.append(code)
                continue
            iso3 = ISO_COUNTRY_CODES.get(iso2)
            iso3_codes.append(iso3 or code)
        country_codes = iso3_codes

    if gadm_gpkg:
        logger.info(f"  GADM GeoPackage: {gadm_gpkg}")
    else:
        logger.info("  No GADM GeoPackage configured; skipping geographic "
                    "resolution (parent assignment, admin concepts).")
    if country_codes:
        logger.info(f"  Country filter:  {country_codes}")

    consolidator = None
    try:
        consolidator = DataConsolidator()

        # 1. Pre-flight: fill any remaining NULL country codes from
        #    coordinates before the main consolidation pass.
        try:
            fixed = consolidator.fix_location_country_codes()
            logger.info(f"  Pre-flight: filled country codes for {fixed} locations")
        except Exception as e:
            # Non-fatal: consolidation will try again inside consolidate_all.
            logger.warning(f"  Pre-flight country-code fill failed: {e}")

        # 2. Single consolidation pass. The previous implementation called
        #    consolidate_all() twice — once with GADM args, once without —
        #    which discarded the GADM work and doubled the runtime.
        results = consolidator.consolidate_all(
            gadm_gpkg=gadm_gpkg,
            country_codes=country_codes,
        )

        # 3. Log a summary using the keys consolidate_all actually returns.
        _log_consolidation_summary(results)

        return results

    except Exception as e:
        logger.error(f"Consolidation failed: {e}", exc_info=True)
        return {"error": str(e)}
    finally:
        if consolidator is not None:
            consolidator.close()


def _log_consolidation_summary(results: Dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    """Emit a human-readable summary of a consolidate_all() result dict."""
    logger.info("-" * 60)
    logger.info("CONSOLIDATION SUMMARY")
    logger.info("-" * 60)

    # Locations
    loc = results.get("locations", {})
    if isinstance(loc, dict):
        original = loc.get("original_count", 0)
        grid     = loc.get("grid_count", 0)
        non_grid = loc.get("non_grid_count", 0)
        clusters = loc.get("cluster_results", {}) or {}
        merged   = clusters.get("merged_count", 0) if isinstance(clusters, dict) else 0
        name_dups = loc.get("name_duplicate_groups", []) or []
        final_fix = loc.get("final_country_fixes", 0)

        logger.info(
            f"  Locations:  {original} total, {grid} grid (preserved), "
            f"{non_grid} non-grid"
        )
        logger.info(f"              {len(name_dups)} name-duplicate groups merged")
        logger.info(f"              {merged} proximity clusters merged")
        logger.info(f"              {final_fix} final country-code fixes")

    # Foreign keys
    fk = results.get("foreign_key_alignment", {})
    if isinstance(fk, dict):
        summary = fk.get("summary", {}) or {}
        logger.info(
            f"  Foreign keys: {summary.get('total_fixed', 0)} rows fixed, "
            f"{summary.get('orphans_found', 0)} orphans found"
        )

    # Vocabulary standardisation (raw value -> concept)
    vstd = results.get("vocabulary_standard", {}) or {}
    for key, stats in vstd.items():
        if isinstance(stats, dict):
            logger.info(
                f"  Standardised {key}: {stats.get('matched', 0)}/"
                f"{stats.get('total', 0)} matched "
                f"({stats.get('exact', 0)} exact, "
                f"{stats.get('normalized', 0)} normalized, "
                f"{stats.get('fuzzy', 0)} fuzzy)"
            )

    # Vocabulary consolidation (duplicate concept merging)
    vcons = results.get("vocabulary_consolidate", {}) or {}
    for table, stats in vcons.items():
        if isinstance(stats, dict):
            dup_count = stats.get("duplicates_found", 0)
            if dup_count > 0:
                logger.info(f"  Merged {dup_count} duplicate groups in {table}")

    # Fact-table duplicates found (pre-resolution)
    dups = results.get("duplicates", {}) or {}
    for table, stats in dups.items():
        if isinstance(stats, dict):
            dup_count = stats.get("duplicate_count", 0)
            if dup_count > 0:
                logger.warning(f"  Found {dup_count} duplicate groups in {table}")

    # Country code validation
    validation = results.get("validation", {}) or {}
    if validation:
        logger.info(
            f"  Country codes: {validation.get('valid', 0)} valid, "
            f"{validation.get('invalid', 0)} invalid, "
            f"{validation.get('missing', 0)} missing "
            f"(of {validation.get('total', 0)} locations)"
        )

    # Hierarchy
    hierarchy = results.get("hierarchy", {}) or {}
    if hierarchy:
        logger.info(
            f"  Hierarchy: {hierarchy.get('placed', 0)}/"
            f"{hierarchy.get('total_non_admin', 0)} non-admin locations "
            f"placed under a parent"
        )

    # Remaining orphans
    orphans = results.get("remaining_orphans", {}) or {}
    if orphans:
        logger.warning(f"  Remaining orphans: {len(orphans)} FK(s)")
        for key, count in orphans.items():
            logger.warning(f"    {key}: {count}")

    # Burden
    burden_fixed = results.get("burden_fixed", 0)
    if burden_fixed:
        logger.info(f"  Burden records repaired: {burden_fixed}")

    logger.info("-" * 60)


def run_burden_expansion(force: bool = True) -> bool:
    """
    Expand yearly disease-burden rows into daily case records.

    Delegates to DataConsolidator.expand_burden_to_daily(), which is the
    single implementation shared with consolidate_all() and the
    fix_cases.py CLI.

    `force=True` matches the caller's intent: this entry point exists for
    the case where consolidation was skipped or is being re-run, so any
    previous ESTIMATE rows should be replaced.
    """
    logger.info("=" * 60)
    logger.info("RUNNING BURDEN EXPANSION")
    logger.info("=" * 60)

    from utilities.consolidation import DataConsolidator

    consolidator = None
    try:
        consolidator = DataConsolidator()
        stats = consolidator.expand_burden_to_daily(force=force)
        logger.info(
            f"Burden expansion completed: "
            f"{stats['inserted']} inserted, "
            f"{stats['already_present']} already present, "
            f"{stats['skipped']} skipped, "
            f"{stats['errors']} errors"
        )
        return True
    except Exception as e:
        logger.error(f"Burden expansion failed: {e}", exc_info=True)
        return False
    finally:
        if consolidator is not None:
            consolidator.close()
            

def generate_summary(output_dir: str = "outputs"):
    """Generate database summary reports."""
    logger.info("Generating database summary...")
    output_path = PROJECT_ROOT / output_dir
    output_path.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
    # Schema summary
    schema_csv = output_path / "schema_summary.csv"
    schema_summary_to_csv(str(schema_csv))
    # Table samples
    samples_dir = output_path / "table_samples"
    export_all_table_samples(str(samples_dir), rows_per_table=100)
    logger.info(f"Summary generated in {output_path}")


def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="CDM Integration Framework Orchestrator")
    parser.add_argument("--config", default="base_config.yaml", help="Path to YAML configuration file")
    parser.add_argument("--skip-ingest", action="store_true", help="Skip data ingestion")
    parser.add_argument("--skip-summary", action="store_true", help="Skip database summary generation")
    parser.add_argument("--skip-consolidation", action="store_true", help="Skip data consolidation")
    parser.add_argument("--expand-burden", action="store_true", help="Force burden expansion before analysis")
    parser.add_argument("--source", help="Run only for a specific source name (optional)")
    args = parser.parse_args()
    
    setup_environment()
    
    # Load configuration
    config_path = PROJECT_ROOT / args.config
    if not config_path.exists():
        logger.error(f"Configuration file not found: {config_path}")
        sys.exit(1)
    
    config = load_yaml_config(str(config_path))
    logger.info(f"Loaded configuration from {config_path}")
    
    # Extract process steps
    process_cfg = config.get('process', {})
    steps = process_cfg.get('steps', [])
    
    # Determine which steps to run
    run_ingest = not args.skip_ingest and 'ingest_data' in steps
    run_consolidate = not args.skip_consolidation and 'consolidate' in steps
    run_summary = not args.skip_summary and 'generate_summary' in steps
    
    
    # --- 1. Ingest data ---
    if run_ingest:
        data_sources = config.get('data_sources', {})
        if not data_sources:
            logger.warning("No data sources defined in configuration.")
        else:
            for source_name, source_cfg in data_sources.items():
                if args.source and source_name != args.source:
                    continue
                run_ingestion_for_source(source_cfg)
    
    # 1.5. Consolidate data
    core = config.get("core", {})
    if run_consolidate:
        result = run_consolidation_step(core)
        if isinstance(result, dict) and result.get("error"):
            logger.error(
                "Consolidation returned an error; skipping summary generation."
            )
            sys.exit(2)
    
    # --- 2. Generate summary ---
    if run_summary:
        generate_summary()
    
    # --- 2.5. Force burden expansion if requested ---
    if args.expand_burden and not run_consolidate:
        logger.info("Force expanding burden data...")
        run_burden_expansion()
    


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

# ==============================================================
# File: scripts/explore_data.py
