#-*- coding:utf-8 -*-

import os
import pandas as pd
from sqlalchemy import inspect, text
from database.session import engine, DATABASE_URL


def schema_summary_to_csv(output_csv: str = "outputs/data/schema_summary.csv", schema_name: str = None) -> pd.DataFrame:
    inspector = inspect(engine)
    if schema_name:
        tables = inspector.get_table_names(schema=schema_name)
    else:
        tables = inspector.get_table_names()
    summary = []
    with engine.connect() as conn:
        for table in tables:
            table_info = {
                'table_name': table,
                'schema': schema_name or 'public',
                'column_count': len(inspector.get_columns(table, schema=schema_name)),
                'index_count': len(inspector.get_indexes(table, schema=schema_name)),
                'foreign_key_count': len(inspector.get_foreign_keys(table, schema=schema_name)),
            }
            try:
                if schema_name:
                    result = conn.execute(text(f'SELECT COUNT(*) FROM {schema_name}.{table}')).scalar()
                else:
                    if DATABASE_URL.startswith("sqlite"):
                        result = conn.execute(text(f'SELECT COUNT(*) FROM {table}')).scalar()
                    else:
                        result = conn.execute(text(f'SELECT COUNT(*) FROM {table}')).scalar()
                table_info['row_count'] = result
            except Exception as e:
                table_info['row_count'] = None
                table_info['row_count_error'] = str(e)
            if DATABASE_URL.startswith("postgresql"):
                try:
                    size_result = conn.execute(text(f"""
                        SELECT pg_size_pretty(pg_total_relation_size('{table}')) as total_size
                    """)).scalar()
                    table_info['total_size'] = size_result
                except:
                    table_info['total_size'] = None
            elif DATABASE_URL.startswith("mysql"):
                try:
                    size_result = conn.execute(text(f"""
                        SELECT ROUND((DATA_LENGTH + INDEX_LENGTH) / 1024 / 1024, 2) as total_size_mb
                        FROM information_schema.TABLES
                        WHERE TABLE_NAME = '{table}'
                    """)).scalar()
                    table_info['total_size_mb'] = size_result
                except:
                    table_info['total_size_mb'] = None
            summary.append(table_info)
    df = pd.DataFrame(summary)
    if output_csv:
        df.to_csv(output_csv, index=False)
        print(f"Schema summary exported to {output_csv}")
    return df


def detailed_table_info(table_name: str, schema_name: str = None, output_csv: str = None) -> pd.DataFrame:
    inspector = inspect(engine)
    columns = inspector.get_columns(table_name, schema=schema_name)
    df = pd.DataFrame(columns)
    if 'type' in df.columns:
        df['data_type'] = df['type'].astype(str)
        df = df.drop('type', axis=1)
    if output_csv:
        df.to_csv(output_csv, index=False)
        print(f"Detailed info for table '{table_name}' exported to {output_csv}")
    return df


def compare_schemas(output_csv: str = "schema_comparison.csv") -> pd.DataFrame:
    current_summary = schema_summary_to_csv(output_csv=None)
    prev_csv = "schema_summary_previous.csv"
    if os.path.exists(prev_csv):
        previous_summary = pd.read_csv(prev_csv)
        comparison = current_summary.merge(
            previous_summary, 
            on='table_name', 
            suffixes=('_current', '_previous')
        )
        comparison['row_count_change'] = comparison['row_count_current'] - comparison['row_count_previous']
        comparison['has_changed'] = (
            (comparison['row_count_change'] != 0) |
            (comparison['column_count_current'] != comparison['column_count_previous'])
        )
        comparison.to_csv(output_csv, index=False)
        print(f"Schema comparison exported to {output_csv}")
        return comparison
    current_summary.to_csv(prev_csv, index=False)
    print(f"No previous schema found. Saved current as baseline in {prev_csv}")
    return current_summary


def export_all_table_samples(output_dir: str = "table_samples", rows_per_table: int = 100):
    os.makedirs(output_dir, exist_ok=True)
    inspector = inspect(engine)
    tables = inspector.get_table_names()
    with engine.connect() as conn:
        for table in tables:
            try:
                df = pd.read_sql(text(f'SELECT * FROM {table} LIMIT {rows_per_table}'), conn)
                output_path = os.path.join(output_dir, f"{table}_sample.csv")
                df.to_csv(output_path, index=False)
                print(f"Exported {len(df)} rows from '{table}' to {output_path}")
            except Exception as e:
                print(f"Error exporting {table}: {e}")


def get_database_metadata() -> pd.DataFrame:
    inspector = inspect(engine)    
    metadata = []
    with engine.connect() as conn:
        for table in inspector.get_table_names():
            pk = inspector.get_pk_constraint(table)
            fks = inspector.get_foreign_keys(table)
            indexes = inspector.get_indexes(table)
            metadata.append({
                'table_name': table,
                'primary_key': pk.get('constrained_columns', []),
                'foreign_keys': [(fk['constrained_columns'], fk['referred_table'], fk['referred_columns']) 
                                for fk in fks],
                'indexes': [idx['column_names'] for idx in indexes],
                'unique_constraints': [idx['column_names'] for idx in indexes if idx.get('unique', False)]
            })
    return pd.DataFrame(metadata)

# ==============================================================
# File: utilities/data_filter_library.py
# ==============================================================
import xarray as xr
import geopandas as gpd
from shapely.geometry import mapping
import os
import tempfile
import pandas as pd
from database.session import SessionLocal
from database.models import DimTime
import pandas as pd
import traceback
import cfgrib
import xarray as xr
import cfgrib
import dask


def clip_era5_by_shapefile(grib_file_path, shapefile_path, filter_params=None, 
                          output_dir='./clipped_data', buffer_degrees=0.5):
    """
    Clip ERA5-Land GRIB files to features from any shapefile.
    (parameters unchanged)
    """
    os.makedirs(output_dir, exist_ok=True)

    def load_shapefile_features():
        """Load and filter features from shapefile (uses closure for filter_params, shapefile_path)"""
        try:
            gdf = gpd.read_file(shapefile_path)
            if gdf.crs is None:
                gdf = gdf.set_crs('EPSG:4326', allow_override=True)
            print(f"Shapefile loaded: {len(gdf)} features")
            print(f"Available columns: {list(gdf.columns)}")

            if filter_params is None:
                return gdf

            if isinstance(filter_params, dict):
                column = filter_params.get('column')
                value = filter_params.get('value')
                if column not in gdf.columns:
                    raise ValueError(f"Column '{column}' not found in shapefile")
                filtered_gdf = gdf[gdf[column] == value]

            elif isinstance(filter_params, list):
                masks = []
                for param in filter_params:
                    column = param.get('column')
                    value = param.get('value')
                    if column not in gdf.columns:
                        print(f"Warning: Column '{column}' not found, skipping")
                        continue
                    masks.append(gdf[column] == value)
                if not masks:
                    raise ValueError("No valid filter conditions found")
                combined_mask = masks[0]
                for mask in masks[1:]:
                    combined_mask = combined_mask | mask
                filtered_gdf = gdf[combined_mask]
            else:
                raise ValueError("filter_params must be dict, list of dicts, or None")

            print(f"Filtered to {len(filtered_gdf)} feature(s)")
            return filtered_gdf
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"Error loading shapefile: {e}")
            raise

    
    def process_feature(feature_gdf, feature_name, grib_file_path, output_dir):
        print(f"\nProcessing {feature_name}...")
        bbox = feature_gdf.total_bounds
        lon_slice = slice(bbox[0] - buffer_degrees, bbox[2] + buffer_degrees)
        lat_slice = slice(bbox[3] + buffer_degrees, bbox[1] - buffer_degrees)

        # Smaller chunk size to control memory
        chunk_size = 10  # time steps per chunk

        # Define daily aggregation methods per variable
        agg_methods = {
            't2m': 'mean',   # 2m temperature → daily mean
            'tp': 'sum',      # total precipitation → daily sum
        }

        try:
            # Open all variable groups lazily
            print("Opening GRIB file with lazy loading...")
            ds_list = cfgrib.open_datasets(
                grib_file_path,
                chunks={'time': chunk_size},
                backend_kwargs={'errors': 'raise'}
            )
            print(f"Found {len(ds_list)} separate variable groups.")
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"Error opening GRIB file: {e}")
            return None

        # Generate output filename
        base_name = os.path.basename(grib_file_path).replace('.grib', '').replace('.grb', '')
        safe_feature_name = feature_name.replace(' ', '_').replace('/', '_')
        output_path = os.path.join(output_dir, f"{base_name}_{safe_feature_name}.nc")

        daily_datasets = []  # store daily versions of each variable

        for i, ds in enumerate(ds_list):
            print(f"  Processing group {i+1}: {list(ds.data_vars)}")
            # Determine dimension names (CF convention)
            lon_dim = 'longitude' if 'longitude' in ds.dims else 'lon'
            lat_dim = 'latitude' if 'latitude' in ds.dims else 'lat'
            # Subset to bounding box
            ds_sel = ds.sel(**{lon_dim: lon_slice, lat_dim: lat_slice})

            # Ensure CRS for clipping
            ds_sel = ds_sel.rio.write_crs("EPSG:4326")
            # Clip to feature boundary
            try:
                clipped = ds_sel.rio.clip(
                    feature_gdf.geometry.apply(mapping),
                    feature_gdf.crs,
                    all_touched=True,
                    drop=True
                )
            except Exception as e:
                print(f"Error during clipping for group {i+1}: {e}")
                continue

            # For each variable in this group, aggregate to daily
            for var in clipped.data_vars:
                method_name = agg_methods.get(var, 'mean')
                print(f"    Aggregating {var} to daily using '{method_name}'...")
                resampler = clipped[var].resample(time='1D')
                daily = getattr(resampler, method_name)()
                daily_ds = daily.to_dataset()
                daily_datasets.append(daily_ds)

        if not daily_datasets:
            print("No data after processing.")
            return None

        # Merge all daily datasets (they now share the same time dimension)
        print("Merging daily datasets...")
        merged = xr.merge(daily_datasets, compat='override', join='outer')
        print(f"Merged dataset variables: {list(merged.data_vars)}")

        # Save with compression
        try:
            encoding = {var: {'zlib': True, 'complevel': 1, 'dtype': 'float32'}
                        for var in merged.data_vars}
            merged.to_netcdf(output_path, encoding=encoding, compute=True)
            print(f"Success! Output saved to {output_path}")
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"Error saving file: {e}")
            return None

        return output_path


    # --- Main flow of clip_era5_by_shapefile ---
    filtered_gdf = load_shapefile_features()
    if filtered_gdf.empty:
        print("No features found after filtering")
        return []

    output_files = []
    for idx, row in filtered_gdf.iterrows():
        single_feature_gdf = gpd.GeoDataFrame([row], geometry='geometry', crs=filtered_gdf.crs)

        # Try to create a meaningful feature name
        feature_name = None
        for name_col in ['NAME', 'name', 'COUNTY', 'county', 'ADM2_NAME', 'ADM1_NAME', 'adm1_name']:
            if name_col in row.index and pd.notna(row[name_col]):
                feature_name = str(row[name_col])
                break
        if feature_name is None:
            feature_name = f"feature_{idx}"
        for id_col in ['ID', 'id', 'OBJECTID', 'FID']:
            if id_col in row.index and pd.notna(row[id_col]):
                feature_name = f"{feature_name}_{row[id_col]}"
                break

        output_file = process_feature(single_feature_gdf, feature_name, grib_file_path, output_dir)
        if output_file:
            output_files.append(output_file)

    return output_files    


def clip_era5_to_region(grib_file_path, geometry, region_name, output_dir='./clipped_data'):
    """
    Clip ERA5 data to a custom geometry (GeoDataFrame, GeoSeries, or Shapely geometry).
    
    Parameters:
    -----------
    grib_file_path : str
        Path to the input GRIB file
    geometry : geopandas.GeoDataFrame, geopandas.GeoSeries, or shapely.geometry
        Geometry to clip to
    region_name : str
        Name for the output file
    output_dir : str
        Directory to save clipped NetCDF file
    
    Returns:
    --------
    str
        Path to saved clipped file
    """
    
    import pandas as pd
    
    os.makedirs(output_dir, exist_ok=True)
    
    # Convert geometry to GeoDataFrame if needed
    if isinstance(geometry, gpd.GeoDataFrame):
        gdf = geometry
    elif isinstance(geometry, gpd.GeoSeries):
        gdf = gpd.GeoDataFrame(geometry=geometry)
    else:
        # Assume it's a shapely geometry
        gdf = gpd.GeoDataFrame(geometry=[geometry], crs='EPSG:4326')
    
    # Ensure CRS is set
    if gdf.crs is None:
        gdf = gdf.set_crs('EPSG:4326', allow_override=True)
    
    # Create a temporary shapefile
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmpdir:
        temp_shapefile = os.path.join(tmpdir, 'temp_region.shp')
        gdf.to_file(temp_shapefile)
        
        # Use the general function with no filtering
        output_files = clip_era5_by_shapefile(
            grib_file_path=grib_file_path,
            shapefile_path=temp_shapefile,
            filter_params=None,  # Use all features (just one in this case)
            output_dir=output_dir
        )
    
    # Rename the output file to use the region_name
    if output_files:
        original_path = output_files[0]
        base_name = os.path.basename(grib_file_path).replace('.grib', '').replace('.grb', '')
        new_path = os.path.join(output_dir, f"{base_name}_{region_name.replace(' ', '_')}.nc")
        
        # Rename the file
        if os.path.exists(original_path):
            os.rename(original_path, new_path)
            return new_path
    
    return None


def batch_clip_era5(grib_files, shapefile_path, filter_params=None, output_base_dir='./clipped_data'):
    """
    Batch clip multiple GRIB files using the same shapefile and filters.
    
    Parameters:
    -----------
    grib_files : list of str
        Paths to GRIB files
    shapefile_path : str
        Path to the shapefile
    filter_params : dict or list of dicts, optional
        Filter parameters (same as clip_era5_by_shapefile)
    output_base_dir : str
        Base directory for output
    
    Returns:
    --------
    dict
        Dictionary mapping input files to lists of output files
    """
    
    results = {}
    
    for grib_file in grib_files:
        print(f"\nProcessing: {grib_file}")
        
        # Create output subdirectory based on input filename
        base_name = os.path.basename(grib_file).replace('.grib', '').replace('.grb', '')
        output_dir = os.path.join(output_base_dir, base_name)
        
        try:
            output_files = clip_era5_by_shapefile(
                grib_file_path=grib_file,
                shapefile_path=shapefile_path,
                filter_params=filter_params,
                output_dir=output_dir
            )
            
            results[grib_file] = output_files
            print(f"Generated {len(output_files)} output files")
            
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"Error processing {grib_file}: {e}")
            results[grib_file] = []
            traceback.print_exc()
    
    return results


def ensure_dates_exist(start_date, end_date):
    session = SessionLocal()
    try:
        date_range = pd.date_range(start_date, end_date)
        for date in date_range:
            date_id = date.strftime('%Y-%m-%d')
            exists = session.query(DimTime).filter_by(date_id=date_id).first()
            if not exists:
                dt = DimTime(
                    date_id=date_id,
                    date=date,
                    year=date.year,
                    month=date.month,
                    week=date.isocalendar()[1],
                    day_of_year=date.timetuple().tm_yday,
                    season='dry' if date.month in [11,12,1,2,3] else 'wet',  # customize for your region
                    is_holiday=False   # was 'no' – now correct boolean
                )
                session.add(dt)
        session.commit()
    except Exception as e:
        session.rollback()
        raise e
    finally:
        session.close()

# File: utilities/data_transform_library.py
# ==============================================================
import os
import gc
import pandas as pd
import numpy as np
from functools import wraps
from typing import Generator, Optional, List, Dict, Any, Union
import warnings
import logging
from rasterio.windows import Window

import rasterio

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

# ========================= Helper Decorators =========================

def ensure_file_exists():
    """Decorator to ensure the input file exists before calling the function."""
    def decorator(func):
        @wraps(func)
        def wrapper(fp, *args, **kwargs):
            if not os.path.exists(fp):
                raise FileNotFoundError(f"File not found: {fp}")
            return func(fp, *args, **kwargs)
        return wrapper
    return decorator


def validate_extension(*valid_extensions: str):
    """
    Decorator to validate file extension before calling the function.
    Also checks file existence.
    """
    def decorator(func):
        @wraps(func)
        def wrapper(fp: str, *args, **kwargs):
            if not os.path.exists(fp):
                raise FileNotFoundError(f"File not found: {fp}")
            ext = os.path.splitext(fp)[1].lower().lstrip('.')
            if ext not in valid_extensions:
                raise ValueError(
                    f"Invalid file extension. Expected {valid_extensions}, got .{ext}"
                )
            return func(fp, *args, **kwargs)
        return wrapper
    return decorator


# ========================= CSV Utilities =========================

def optimized_df_to_csv(df: pd.DataFrame, output_path: str, chunksize: int = 100000) -> bool:
    """
    Write a DataFrame to CSV in chunks to save memory.
    Returns True if successful, False if df is empty or None.
    """
    if df is None or df.empty:
        return False
    if chunksize >= len(df):
        df.to_csv(output_path, index=False)
    else:
        with open(output_path, 'w', encoding='utf-8') as f:
            for i in range(0, len(df), chunksize):
                chunk = df.iloc[i:i+chunksize]
                chunk.to_csv(f, index=False, header=(i == 0), mode='a')
    return True


# ========================= Raster / GeoTIFF / COG =========================

def _raster_to_df(
    fp: str,
    band: Optional[Union[int, List[int]]] = None,
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """
    Internal: open any raster supported by rioxarray and return as DataFrame.

    Args:
        fp:   Path to the raster file.
        band: Band selection, using 1-based indexing (matching GDAL).
              None    -> all bands (one row per pixel per band).
              int     -> a single band, e.g. band=1 for the mean of a
                         Mean/LCI/UCI triple.
              list    -> multiple bands.

    Returns:
        Long-format DataFrame with at least x, y, value columns. When
        `band` is None and the raster has more than one band, a `band`
        column is also present.
    """
    import rioxarray
    raster = rioxarray.open_rasterio(fp, chunks='auto')

    n_bands = raster.sizes.get('band', 1)

    if band is not None:
        if isinstance(band, int):
            if band < 1 or band > n_bands:
                raise ValueError(
                    f"band={band} out of range; file has {n_bands} band(s)"
                )
            # drop=True removes the scalar band coordinate so the resulting
            # DataFrame has no `band` column.
            raster = raster.sel(band=band, drop=True)
        elif isinstance(band, (list, tuple)):
            b = list(band)
            if not all(isinstance(x, int) and 1 <= x <= n_bands for x in b):
                raise ValueError(
                    f"band list {b} out of range; file has {n_bands} band(s)"
                )
            raster = raster.sel(band=b)
        else:
            raise ValueError(
                f"band must be int, list, or None; got {type(band).__name__}"
            )

    df = raster.to_dataframe(name='value').reset_index()
    if hasattr(raster, '_file_obj') and raster._file_obj:
        raster._file_obj.close()
    del raster
    gc.collect()
    return df


@validate_extension('cog', 'tif', 'tiff')
def cog_to_df(
    fp: str,
    band: Optional[Union[int, List[int]]] = None,
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Convert Cloud Optimized GeoTIFF (COG) or regular GeoTIFF to DataFrame."""
    return _raster_to_df(fp, band=band)


@validate_extension('cog', 'tif', 'tiff')
def cog_to_csv(
    fp: str,
    csv_path: str,
    chunksize: int = 50000,
    band: Optional[Union[int, List[int]]] = None,
) -> bool:
    """Convert COG/TIFF directly to CSV."""
    df = cog_to_df(fp, band=band)
    return optimized_df_to_csv(df, csv_path, chunksize)


@validate_extension('tif', 'tiff')
def tif_to_df_chunked(
    fp: str,
    window_size: int = 512,
    nodata_threshold: float = -9999.0,
    band: int = 1,
) -> Generator[pd.DataFrame, None, None]:
    """
    Generator that yields DataFrames for each block of a GeoTIFF.
    Filters out NoData values (<= nodata_threshold).
    Yields DataFrames with columns: 'x', 'y', 'value' (single band).

    Args:
        fp:               Path to the GeoTIFF.
        window_size:      Window size in pixels.
        nodata_threshold: Values <= this are dropped. Default -9999.
        band:             1-based band index to read. Default 1.
    """
    import rasterio
    from rasterio.windows import Window
    import numpy as np
    import pandas as pd

    with rasterio.open(fp) as src:
        if band < 1 or band > src.count:
            raise ValueError(
                f"band={band} out of range; file has {src.count} band(s)"
            )

        height, width = src.height, src.width
        nodata = src.nodata if src.nodata is not None else nodata_threshold

        for row_off in range(0, height, window_size):
            for col_off in range(0, width, window_size):
                win = Window(col_off, row_off,
                             min(window_size, width - col_off),
                             min(window_size, height - row_off))
                data = src.read(band, window=win)  # 2D array
                if data.size == 0:
                    continue

                rows, cols = data.shape
                col_indices = np.arange(cols)
                row_indices = np.arange(rows)
                xs, ys = src.transform * np.meshgrid(col_indices, row_indices)

                flat_xs   = xs.flatten()
                flat_ys   = ys.flatten()
                flat_vals = data.flatten()

                mask = flat_vals > nodata_threshold
                if not np.any(mask):
                    continue

                yield pd.DataFrame({
                    'x': flat_xs[mask],
                    'y': flat_ys[mask],
                    'value': flat_vals[mask],
                })


@validate_extension('tif', 'tiff')
def tif_to_df(
    fp: str,
    band: Optional[Union[int, List[int]]] = None,
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Alias for cog_to_df."""
    return cog_to_df(fp, band=band)


@validate_extension('tif', 'tiff')
def tif_to_csv(
    fp: str,
    csv_path: str,
    chunksize: int = 50000,
    band: Optional[Union[int, List[int]]] = None,
) -> bool:
    """Alias for cog_to_csv."""
    return cog_to_csv(fp, csv_path, chunksize, band=band)
    

# ========================= NetCDF (including .nc and .nc4) =========================

def _netcdf_to_df(fp: str, variable: Optional[str] = None, time_idx: int = 0) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """
    Internal: open NetCDF/GRIB and return a DataFrame for a specific variable and time step.
    If variable is None, uses the first data variable.
    """
    import xarray as xr
    if fp.lower().endswith(('.grib', '.grib2')):
        ds = xr.open_dataset(fp, engine='cfgrib', chunks='auto')
    else:
        ds = xr.open_dataset(fp, chunks='auto')

    if variable is None:
        variable = list(ds.data_vars.keys())[0]
        warnings.warn(f"No variable specified, using '{variable}'")

    da = ds[variable]
    if 'time' in da.dims:
        da = da.isel(time=time_idx)
    df = da.to_dataframe().reset_index()
    ds.close()
    gc.collect()
    return df


@validate_extension('nc', 'nc4')
def nc_to_df(fp: str, variable: Optional[str] = None, time_idx: int = 0) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Convert NetCDF (.nc or .nc4) to DataFrame, optionally selecting variable and time index."""
    return _netcdf_to_df(fp, variable, time_idx)


@validate_extension('nc', 'nc4')
def nc_to_csv(fp: str, csv_path: str, chunksize: int = 100000,
              variable: Optional[str] = None, time_idx: int = 0) -> bool:
    """Convert NetCDF directly to CSV."""
    df = nc_to_df(fp, variable, time_idx)
    return optimized_df_to_csv(df, csv_path, chunksize)


# Keep for backward compatibility
nc4_to_df = nc_to_df
nc4_to_csv = nc_to_csv


# ========================= GRIB =========================

@validate_extension('grib', 'grib2')
def grib_to_df(fp: str, variable: Optional[str] = None, time_idx: int = 0) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Convert GRIB file to DataFrame (uses cfgrib engine)."""
    return _netcdf_to_df(fp, variable, time_idx)


@validate_extension('grib', 'grib2')
def grib_to_csv(fp: str, csv_path: str, chunksize: int = 100000,
                variable: Optional[str] = None, time_idx: int = 0) -> bool:
    """Convert GRIB directly to CSV."""
    df = grib_to_df(fp, variable, time_idx)
    return optimized_df_to_csv(df, csv_path, chunksize)


def _get_engine(fp: str) -> Optional[str]:
    """Return 'cfgrib' for GRIB files, otherwise None for auto-detection."""
    if fp.lower().endswith(('.grib', '.grib2')):
        return 'cfgrib'
    return None


def _get_time_dim(ds) -> str:
    """Return the name of the time dimension (supports 'time', 't', 'Time')."""
    for candidate in ['time', 't', 'Time']:
        if candidate in ds.dims:
            return candidate
    return None


def timeseries_to_df_chunked(
    fp: str,
    variables: Optional[List[str]] = None,
    time_chunk_size: int = 100,
    output_csv: Optional[str] = None
):
    """
    Process timeseries data in chunks along the time dimension.
    
    Args:
        fp: Path to NetCDF or GRIB file
        variables: List of variable names (None = all variables)
        time_chunk_size: Number of time steps per chunk
        output_csv: If provided, write all chunks to this CSV file (no DataFrame returned).
                    If None, yields one DataFrame per chunk.
    
    Yields:
        DataFrame for each time chunk (if output_csv is None)
    Returns:
        bool (if output_csv provided) indicating success
    """
    import xarray as xr
    engine = _get_engine(fp)
    
    # Open dataset with chunking along time dimension
    ds = xr.open_dataset(fp, engine=engine, chunks={'time': time_chunk_size})
    
    if variables is None:
        # Skip auxiliary variables that xarray reports as data_vars
        # but are not geophysical fields: crs, spatial_ref, expver,
        # time_bnds, lat_bnds, lon_bnds, height, number, surface, depth.
        # The `ndim >= 2` clause catches anything else that isn't a
        # lat/lon field regardless of its name.
        _AUX = {"crs", "spatial_ref", "expver", "number", "surface",
                "height", "depth", "time_bnds", "lat_bnds", "lon_bnds"}
        variables = [
            v for v in ds.data_vars
            if v.lower() not in _AUX and ds[v].ndim >= 2
        ]
    
    time_dim = _get_time_dim(ds)
    if time_dim is None:
        # No time dimension – treat whole dataset as one chunk
        n_time = 1
        time_dim = None
    else:
        n_time = ds.dims[time_dim]
    
    csv_file = None
    try:
        if output_csv:
            csv_file = open(output_csv, 'w', encoding='utf-8', newline='')
        
        # Iterate over time chunks (or single chunk if no time dimension)
        if time_dim is None:
            ranges = [(0, 1)]
        else:
            ranges = [(start, min(start + time_chunk_size, n_time))
                      for start in range(0, n_time, time_chunk_size)]
        
        for start, end in ranges:
            if time_dim is not None:
                ds_chunk = ds.isel({time_dim: slice(start, end)})
            else:
                ds_chunk = ds
            
            # Build long-format DataFrame for this chunk
            chunk_dfs = []
            for var in variables:
                df = ds_chunk[var].to_dataframe().reset_index()
                df['variable'] = var
                df = df.rename(columns={var: 'value'})
                # Keep only dimension columns + variable + value
                dims = list(ds_chunk.dims.keys())
                keep_cols = [col for col in df.columns if col in dims] + ['variable', 'value']
                df = df[keep_cols]
                chunk_dfs.append(df)
            
            chunk_df = pd.concat(chunk_dfs, ignore_index=True)
            
            if output_csv:
                mode = 'w' if csv_file.tell() == 0 else 'a'
                header = (mode == 'w')
                chunk_df.to_csv(csv_file, index=False, mode=mode, header=header)
            else:
                yield chunk_df
            
            del chunk_df, chunk_dfs, ds_chunk
            gc.collect()
        
        if output_csv:
            logger.info(f"Timeseries data written to {output_csv} (total time steps: {n_time})")
            return True
    
    except Exception as e:
        if output_csv and csv_file:
            csv_file.close()
        raise e
    finally:
        ds.close()
        if output_csv and csv_file:
            csv_file.close()


@validate_extension('nc', 'nc4', 'grib', 'grib2')
def timeseries_to_df(
    fp: str,
    variables: Optional[List[str]] = None,
    warn_large: bool = True,
    max_expected_rows: int = 10_000_000
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """
    Extract all time steps for given variables into a single DataFrame.
    WARNING: Only use for small to medium files. For large files, use timeseries_to_df_chunked.
    
    Args:
        fp: Path to NetCDF or GRIB file
        variables: List of variable names (None = all variables)
        warn_large: If True, show warning when estimated rows exceed max_expected_rows
        max_expected_rows: Threshold for warning (default 10 million)
    
    Returns:
        Single DataFrame with all time steps and variables (long format)
    """
    import xarray as xr
    engine = _get_engine(fp)
    ds = xr.open_dataset(fp, engine=engine, chunks='auto')
    
    try:
        if variables is None:
            # Skip auxiliary variables that xarray reports as data_vars
            # but are not geophysical fields: crs, spatial_ref, expver,
            # time_bnds, lat_bnds, lon_bnds, height, number, surface, depth.
            # The `ndim >= 2` clause catches anything else that isn't a
            # lat/lon field regardless of its name.
            _AUX = {"crs", "spatial_ref", "expver", "number", "surface",
                    "height", "depth", "time_bnds", "lat_bnds", "lon_bnds"}
            variables = [
                v for v in ds.data_vars
                if v.lower() not in _AUX and ds[v].ndim >= 2
            ]
        # Estimate size
        time_dim = _get_time_dim(ds)
        n_time = ds.dims[time_dim] if time_dim else 1
        sample_var = ds[variables[0]]
        n_pixels = sample_var.size // n_time if n_time > 1 else sample_var.size
        estimated_rows = n_time * n_pixels * len(variables)
        if warn_large and estimated_rows > max_expected_rows:
            warnings.warn(
                f"Estimated rows: {estimated_rows:,} (> {max_expected_rows:,}). "
                "This may cause memory issues. Use timeseries_to_df_chunked() instead.",
                UserWarning
            )
        dfs = []
        for var in variables:
            df = ds[var].to_dataframe().reset_index()
            df['variable'] = var
            df = df.rename(columns={var: 'value'})
            dims = list(ds.dims.keys())
            keep_cols = [col for col in df.columns if col in dims] + ['variable', 'value']
            df = df[keep_cols]
            dfs.append(df)
        result = pd.concat(dfs, ignore_index=True)
    finally:
        ds.close()
    return result


def timeseries_to_csv(
    fp: str,
    csv_path: str,
    variables: Optional[List[str]] = None,
    time_chunk_size: int = 100
) -> bool:
    """
    Convert timeseries file directly to CSV using chunked writing.
    This is the recommended method for large files.
    """
    return timeseries_to_df_chunked(
        fp=fp,
        variables=variables,
        time_chunk_size=time_chunk_size,
        output_csv=csv_path
    )


# ========================= Statistical Files (SPSS, Stata, SAS) =========================

def _readstat_chunked(fp: str, reader_func, chunksize: int = 50000) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Generic chunked reader for pyreadstat-based formats."""
    import pyreadstat
    reader = reader_func(fp, chunksize=chunksize)
    chunks = []
    for df_chunk, _ in reader:
        chunks.append(df_chunk)
    return pd.concat(chunks, ignore_index=True) if chunks else pd.DataFrame()


@validate_extension('dta')
def dta_to_df(fp: str, chunksize: int = 50000) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Convert Stata .dta file to DataFrame."""
    import pyreadstat
    return _readstat_chunked(fp, lambda f, cs: pyreadstat.read_file_in_chunks(
        pyreadstat.read_dta, f, chunksize=cs), chunksize)


@validate_extension('dta')
def dta_to_csv(fp: str, csv_path: str, chunksize: int = 50000) -> bool:
    df = dta_to_df(fp, chunksize)
    return optimized_df_to_csv(df, csv_path, chunksize)


@validate_extension('sas7bdat')
def sas7bdat_to_df(fp: str, chunksize: int = 50000) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Convert SAS .sas7bdat file to DataFrame."""
    import pyreadstat
    return _readstat_chunked(fp, lambda f, cs: pyreadstat.read_file_in_chunks(
        pyreadstat.read_sas7bdat, f, chunksize=cs), chunksize)


@validate_extension('sas7bdat')
def sas7bdat_to_csv(fp: str, csv_path: str, chunksize: int = 50000) -> bool:
    df = sas7bdat_to_df(fp, chunksize)
    return optimized_df_to_csv(df, csv_path, chunksize)


@validate_extension('sav')
def sav_to_df(fp: str, chunksize: int = 50000) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Convert SPSS .sav file to DataFrame."""
    import pyreadstat
    return _readstat_chunked(fp, lambda f, cs: pyreadstat.read_file_in_chunks(
        pyreadstat.read_sav, f, chunksize=cs), chunksize)


@validate_extension('sav')

    return optimized_df_to_csv(df, csv_path, chunksize)

def _vector_to_df(fp: str, read_geometry: bool = False) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Internal: read vector file with pyogrio (fast) and optionally drop geometry."""
    import pyogrio
    df = pyogrio.read_dataframe(fp, read_geometry=read_geometry)
    return df


@validate_extension('geojson')
def geojson_to_df(fp: str) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Convert GeoJSON to DataFrame (drops geometry)."""
    return _vector_to_df(fp, read_geometry=False)

# ========================= JSON / TSV / Excel / XML =========================

@validate_extension('json')
def json_to_df(fp: str, lines: bool = True, chunksize: int = 50000) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """
    Convert JSON file to DataFrame.
    By default assumes line-delimited JSON (lines=True). For regular JSON, set lines=False.
    """
    reader = pd.read_json(fp, lines=lines, chunksize=chunksize)
    chunks = []
    for chunk in reader:
        chunks.append(chunk)
    return pd.concat(chunks, ignore_index=True) if chunks else pd.DataFrame()


@validate_extension('json')
def json_to_csv(fp: str, csv_path: str, chunksize: int = 50000, lines: bool = True) -> bool:
    df = json_to_df(fp, lines=lines, chunksize=chunksize)
    return optimized_df_to_csv(df, csv_path, chunksize)


@validate_extension('tsv')
def tsv_to_df(fp: str, chunksize: int = 50000, **kwargs) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Convert TSV (tab-separated) file to DataFrame."""
    reader = pd.read_csv(fp, sep='\t', chunksize=chunksize, **kwargs)
    chunks = []
    for chunk in reader:
        chunks.append(chunk)
    return pd.concat(chunks, ignore_index=True) if chunks else pd.DataFrame()


@validate_extension('tsv')
def tsv_to_csv(fp: str, csv_path: str, chunksize: int = 50000, **kwargs) -> bool:
    df = tsv_to_df(fp, chunksize, **kwargs)
    return optimized_df_to_csv(df, csv_path, chunksize)


@validate_extension('xlsx', 'xls')
def xlsx_to_df(fp: str, sheet_name: Union[str, int] = 0, chunksize: int = 50000) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """
    Convert Excel file to DataFrame.

    pandas.read_excel() has no chunksize parameter, unlike read_csv. The
    whole sheet is read in one call. The `chunksize` argument is retained
    in the signature for API symmetry with the other readers; it is not
    passed to pandas and has no effect on the return.

    Uses 'calamine' engine if available (fast), otherwise 'openpyxl'.
    """
    try:
        return pd.read_excel(fp, sheet_name=sheet_name, engine='calamine')
    except ImportError:
        return pd.read_excel(fp, sheet_name=sheet_name, engine='openpyxl')


@validate_extension('xlsx', 'xls')
def xlsx_to_csv(fp: str, csv_path: str, chunksize: int = 50000,
                sheet_name: Union[str, int] = 0) -> bool:
    df = xlsx_to_df(fp, sheet_name=sheet_name, chunksize=chunksize)
    return optimized_df_to_csv(df, csv_path, chunksize)


@validate_extension('xml')
def xml_to_df(fp: str, record_tag: str = 'record', max_rows: Optional[int] = None) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """
    Convert XML file to DataFrame by streaming.
    Assumes a flat structure where each record is an element with child tags as columns.
    """
    import xml.etree.ElementTree as ET
    rows = []
    for event, elem in ET.iterparse(fp, events=('end',)):
        if elem.tag == record_tag:
            row = {child.tag: child.text for child in elem}
            rows.append(row)
            elem.clear()
            if max_rows is not None and len(rows) >= max_rows:
                break
    return pd.DataFrame(rows)


@validate_extension('xml')
def xml_to_csv(fp: str, csv_path: str, chunksize: int = 50000,
               record_tag: str = 'record') -> bool:
    """Convert XML to CSV, reading all records (no row limit)."""
    df = xml_to_df(fp, record_tag=record_tag, max_rows=None)
    return optimized_df_to_csv(df, csv_path, chunksize)


# ========================= YAML Config Loader =========================

@ensure_file_exists()
def load_yaml_config(fp: str) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """Load YAML configuration file into a dictionary."""
    import yaml
    with open(fp, 'r', encoding='utf-8') as f:
        return yaml.safe_load(f)

from utilities.types import ProcessResult


class Extractor(ABC):
    @abstractmethod
    def extract_data(self, ext:str, source_file_path:str) -> ProcessResult:
        pass


class Transformer(ABC):
    @abstractmethod
    def transform(self, ext:str, source_file_path:str, mapping_file_path:str) -> ProcessResult:
        pass 


class Loader(ABC):
    @abstractmethod
    def load(self, source_data:pd.DataFrame, mapping_file_path:str) -> ProcessResult:
        pass

"""
CLI to build all vocabulary CSVs.

Usage:
    python -m utilities.vocabulary.build --gadm path/to/gadm41_KEN.gpkg
                                          --idomal path/to/idomal.obo
                                          --out vocabularies/
"""

import argparse
from pathlib import Path

from utilities.vocabulary.locations import build_location_vocabulary
from utilities.vocabulary.diseases  import build_disease_vocabulary
from utilities.vocabulary.species   import build_species_vocabulary


def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("--gadm",    required=True)
    ap.add_argument("--idomal",  required=False)
    ap.add_argument("--out",     default="vocabularies")
    args = ap.parse_args()

    out = Path(args.out); out.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    print("Building location vocabulary from GADM …")
    build_location_vocabulary(args.gadm, out / "gadm_locations.csv")

    if args.idomal:
        print("Building disease vocabulary from IDOMAL …")
        build_disease_vocabulary(args.idomal, out / "idomal_diseases.csv")

    print("Building species vocabulary from NCBI …")
    build_species_vocabulary(out / "ncbi_vectors.csv")

    print(f"Done. Vocabulary CSVs in {out}/")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

# ==============================================================
# File: utilities/vocabulary/diseases.py
# ==============================================================
"""
Extract disease and malaria terms from IDOMAL (OBO format).

IDOMAL is the malaria ontology, built on BFO and following OBO Foundry rules.
Download: http://purl.obolibrary.org/obo/idomal.obo
"""

import pronto
import pandas as pd
from typing import Optional


def build_disease_vocabulary(obo_path: str, output_csv: str,
                             root_terms: Optional[list] = None) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """
    Extract terms from an OBO ontology.

    Args:
        obo_path: Path to the .obo file (IDOMAL, IDO, MONDO, ...)
        output_csv: Where to write the vocabulary
        root_terms: If provided, only keep descendants of these term IDs.
                    For IDOMAL, e.g. ['IDOMAL:0000000'] for the whole ontology.
    """
    ont = pronto.Ontology(obo_path)

    rows = []
    for term in ont.terms():
        if term.obsolete:
            continue
        if root_terms and not any(term.id in r or r in term.superclasses(distance=100)
                                  for r in root_terms):
            continue

        rows.append({
            "concept_code":  term.id,                     # 'IDOMAL:0000657'
            "concept_name":  term.name or "",             # preferred label
            "concept_class": _infer_class(term),          # 'disease', 'vector', 'intervention'
            "parent_code":   next((p.id for p in term.superclasses(distance=1)), None),
            "synonyms":      "|".join(s.description for s in term.synonyms),
            "definition":    (term.definition or "")[:500],
        })

    df = pd.DataFrame(rows)
    df["vocabulary_id"] = _detect_vocab_id(obo_path)   # 'IDOMAL', 'IDO', 'MONDO'
    df["domain"] = "disease"
    df["name_normalized"] = df["concept_name"].str.lower().str.strip()

    df.to_csv(output_csv, index=False)
    return df


def _infer_class(term) -> str:
    """Crude class inference from the term's ancestor chain."""
    ancestors = {a.name.lower() for a in term.superclasses(distance=100) if a.name}
    if "disease" in ancestors:
        return "disease"
    if "mosquito" in ancestors or "vector" in ancestors:
        return "vector"
    if "insecticide" in ancestors:
        return "insecticide"
    return "term"


def _detect_vocab_id(path: str) -> str:
    name = path.lower()
    if "idomal" in name: return "IDOMAL"
    if "mondo" in name:  return "MONDO"
    if "ido"   in name:  return "IDO"
    if "miro"  in name:  return "MIRO"
    return "UNKNOWN"

# ==============================================================
# File: utilities/vocabulary/loader.py
# ==============================================================
"""
Loads vocabulary CSVs into dim_vocabulary / dim_concept and provides
matching utilities for standardizing raw CDM values.
"""

import re
import unicodedata
from typing import Dict, Optional, Tuple

import pandas as pd
from rapidfuzz import fuzz, process
from sqlalchemy import text


# =============================================================================
# Normalisation helpers
# =============================================================================

_SPECIES_QUALIFIER_RE = re.compile(r"\s+(complex|subgroup|group)\s*$")
_SPECIES_STRAIN_RE    = re.compile(r"\s+(?:s\s*form|m\s*form|form)\b.*$")
_SPECIES_GENUS_RE     = re.compile(r"^anopheles\s+")


def _norm_species(s) -> str:
    """
    Species-specific normalisation.

    Collapses case, underscores/hyphens, the genus prefix, and the common
    qualifiers ('complex', 'subgroup', 'group', 's form', 'm form',
    's.l.', 's.s.') so that, for example:

        'Gambiae Complex'
        'gambiae complex'
        'Anopheles gambiae'
        'gambiae_s form'
        'gambiae_s form_m form'

    all reduce to the single key 'gambiae'.

    Deliberately does NOT collapse hybrid designations such as
    'Anopheles coluzzii/gambiae' — those are legitimately distinct and
    should not be merged with either parent species.
    """
    if s is None:
        return ""
    try:
        if pd.isna(s):
            return ""
    except (TypeError, ValueError):
        pass

    s = str(s).strip().lower()
    s = re.sub(r"[_\-]", " ", s)          # underscores/hyphens -> space
    s = re.sub(r"\s+", " ", s)            # collapse whitespace
    s = _SPECIES_GENUS_RE.sub("", s)      # drop leading 'anopheles '
    s = _SPECIES_QUALIFIER_RE.sub("", s)  # drop trailing complex/subgroup/group
    s = re.sub(r"\s+s\.?\s*l\.?$", "", s) # drop trailing 's.l.'
    s = re.sub(r"\s+s\.?\s*s\.?$", "", s) # drop trailing 's.s.'
    s = _SPECIES_STRAIN_RE.sub("", s)     # drop 's form' / 'm form' / 'form ...'
    return s.strip()


def _norm_deep(s) -> str:
    """
    Generic normalisation used for non-species domains:
    strip diacritics, collapse whitespace, lower-case.
    """
    if s is None:
        return ""
    s = unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", str(s))
    s = "".join(c for c in s if not unicodedata.combining(c))
    return " ".join(s.lower().split())


# =============================================================================
# VocabularyLoader
# =============================================================================

class VocabularyLoader:
    def __init__(self, session):
        self.session = session
        self._cache: Dict[Tuple[str, str], pd.DataFrame] = {}
        # (vocab_id, 'vector') -> {normalised_key: concept_row_dict}
        self._species_index: Dict[Tuple[str, str], Dict[str, dict]] = {}

    # -------------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Loading
    # -------------------------------------------------------------------------

    def load_csv(self, vocab_id: str, path: str, domain: str):
        """Insert a vocabulary CSV into dim_vocabulary + dim_concept."""
        df = pd.read_csv(path)
        df["vocabulary_id"] = vocab_id
        df["domain"] = domain
        df["name_normalized"] = df["concept_name"].str.lower().str.strip()

        # Upsert the vocabulary row.
        self.session.execute(text("""
            INSERT INTO dim_vocabulary (vocabulary_id, vocabulary_name, source_url, version)
            VALUES (:id, :name, :url, :ver)
            ON CONFLICT (vocabulary_id) DO UPDATE SET
                vocabulary_name = EXCLUDED.vocabulary_name,
                version         = EXCLUDED.version
        """), {"id": vocab_id, "name": vocab_id, "url": None, "ver": None})

        # Upsert concepts.
        for _, row in df.iterrows():
            self.session.execute(text("""
                INSERT INTO dim_concept
                    (vocabulary_id, concept_code, concept_name,
                     concept_class, domain, parent_code, extra)
                VALUES (:v, :c, :n, :k, :d, :p, :e)
                ON CONFLICT (vocabulary_id, concept_code) DO UPDATE SET
                    concept_name  = EXCLUDED.concept_name,
                    concept_class = EXCLUDED.concept_class,
                    parent_code   = EXCLUDED.parent_code,
                    extra         = EXCLUDED.extra
            """), {
                "v": vocab_id, "c": str(row["concept_code"]),
                "n": row["concept_name"], "k": row.get("concept_class"),
                "d": domain, "p": row.get("parent_code"),
                "e": row.get("synonyms"),
            })

        self.session.commit()
        self._cache[(vocab_id, domain)] = df
        # Drop any species index built from a previous version of this CSV
        # so it will be rebuilt on next use.
        self._species_index.pop((vocab_id, "vector"), None)

    # -------------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Caches
    # -------------------------------------------------------------------------

    def _get(self, vocab_id: str, domain: str) -> pd.DataFrame:
        key = (vocab_id, domain)
        if key not in self._cache:
            rows = self.session.execute(text("""
                SELECT concept_code, concept_name, concept_class,
                       parent_code, extra AS synonyms
                FROM dim_concept
                WHERE vocabulary_id = :v AND domain = :d
            """), {"v": vocab_id, "d": domain}).fetchall()
            self._cache[key] = pd.DataFrame(rows, columns=[
                "concept_code", "concept_name", "concept_class",
                "parent_code", "synonyms"
            ])
        return self._cache[key]

    def _get_species_index(self, vocab_id: str) -> Dict[str, dict]:
        """
        Lazily build the normalised-key -> concept-row map for the
        vector domain of one vocabulary.

        Both the concept_name and every pipe-separated entry in the
        synonyms column are indexed under _norm_species. First writer
        wins on collision; the vocabulary CSV is assumed to be curated
        so collisions should be rare.
        """
        key = (vocab_id, "vector")
        if key in self._species_index:
            return self._species_index[key]

        df = self._get(vocab_id, "vector")
        idx: Dict[str, dict] = {}
        if not df.empty:
            for _, row in df.iterrows():
                # Index the concept name itself.
                k = _norm_species(row["concept_name"])
                if k and k not in idx:
                    idx[k] = row.to_dict()

                # Index each synonym.
                syns = row.get("synonyms") or ""
                if isinstance(syns, str) and syns:
                    for syn in syns.split("|"):
                        sk = _norm_species(syn)
                        if sk and sk not in idx:
                            idx[sk] = row.to_dict()

        self._species_index[key] = idx
        return idx

    # -------------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Matching
    # -------------------------------------------------------------------------

    def match(self, raw_value: str, vocab_id: str, domain: str,
              threshold: float = 0.85
              ) -> Tuple[Optional[dict], str, float]:
        """
        Match a raw value against a vocabulary.

        Returns (concept_dict_or_None, method, score).

        method is one of:
            'exact'       - case-insensitive exact match on concept_name
            'normalized'  - matched after domain-specific normalisation
                            (species normalisation for the vector domain,
                            diacritic stripping for everything else)
            'fuzzy'       - token_set_ratio above `threshold`
            'none'        - no match
        """
        if not raw_value:
            return None, "none", 0.0

        df = self._get(vocab_id, domain)
        if df.empty:
            return None, "none", 0.0

        # ---- 1. Exact match -------------------------------------------------
        norm = str(raw_value).lower().strip()
        exact = df[df["concept_name"].str.lower().str.strip() == norm]
        if not exact.empty:
            return exact.iloc[0].to_dict(), "exact", 1.0

        # ---- 2a. Species-normalised match (vector domain only) --------------
        if domain == "vector":
            species_key = _norm_species(raw_value)
            if species_key:
                idx = self._get_species_index(vocab_id)
                hit = idx.get(species_key)
                if hit is not None:
                    return hit, "normalized", 0.95

        # ---- 2b. Generic normalised match -----------------------------------
        target = _norm_deep(raw_value)
        normalized = df[df["concept_name"].apply(_norm_deep) == target]
        if not normalized.empty:
            return normalized.iloc[0].to_dict(), "normalized", 0.95

        # ---- 3. Fuzzy match -------------------------------------------------
        choices = df["concept_name"].tolist()
        best = process.extractOne(raw_value, choices, scorer=fuzz.token_set_ratio)
        if best and best[1] >= threshold * 100:
            concept = df[df["concept_name"] == best[0]].iloc[0].to_dict()
            return concept, "fuzzy", best[1] / 100.0

        return None, "none", 0.0


# =============================================================================
# Convenience wrapper
# =============================================================================

def match_value(session, value, vocab_id, domain, threshold=0.85):
    return VocabularyLoader(session).match(value, vocab_id, domain, threshold)

# ==============================================================
# File: utilities/vocabulary/locations.py
# ==============================================================
"""
Build a location vocabulary from GADM administrative boundaries.

GADM 4.1 layout (verified against gadm41_KEN.gpkg):
  - Layers:    ADM_ADM_0, ADM_ADM_1, ADM_ADM_2, ADM_ADM_3
  - Level 0:   name lives in COUNTRY  (no NAME_0)
  - Levels 1+  name lives in NAME_<n>, variant in VARNAME_<n>
"""

import geopandas as gpd
import pandas as pd
from typing import Optional


# Column/class spec per administrative level.
GADM_LEVELS = {
    0: {
        "name_candidates": ["COUNTRY", "NAME_0"],
        "gid_column":      "GID_0",
        "concept_class":   "country",
        "varname_column":  None,
    },
    1: {
        "name_candidates": ["NAME_1"],
        "gid_column":      "GID_1",
        "concept_class":   "admin1",
        "varname_column":  "VARNAME_1",
    },
    2: {
        "name_candidates": ["NAME_2"],
        "gid_column":      "GID_2",
        "concept_class":   "admin2",
        "varname_column":  "VARNAME_2",
    },
    3: {
        "name_candidates": ["NAME_3"],
        "gid_column":      "GID_3",
        "concept_class":   "admin3",
        "varname_column":  "VARNAME_3",
    },
}


def _load_level(gpkg_path: str, level: int, country_code: Optional[str]) -> Optional[gpd.GeoDataFrame]:
    """Try the standard layer name, then the per-country fallback."""
    candidates = [f"ADM_ADM_{level}"]
    if country_code:
        candidates.append(f"gadm41_{country_code}_{level}")
    for layer in candidates:
        try:
            return gpd.read_file(gpkg_path, layer=layer)
        except Exception:
            continue
    return None


def _first_present(df: pd.DataFrame, columns):
    """Return the first column name in `columns` that exists in `df`."""
    for col in columns:
        if col in df.columns:
            return col
    return None


def build_location_vocabulary(
    gpkg_path: str,
    output_csv: str,
    country_codes: Optional[list] = None,
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """
    Extract location concepts from a GADM GeoPackage.

    Produces one row per administrative unit with:
        concept_code, concept_name, concept_class, parent_code,
        centroid_lat, centroid_lon, synonyms, vocabulary_id, domain,
        name_normalized

    Parameters
    ----------
    gpkg_path : str
        Path to the GADM GeoPackage (e.g. gadm41_KEN.gpkg).
    output_csv : str
        Where to write the resulting vocabulary.
    country_codes : list[str] | None
        Optional GID_0 filter, e.g. ['KEN'].
    """
    rows = []
    cc0 = country_codes[0] if country_codes else None

    for level, spec in GADM_LEVELS.items():
        gdf = _load_level(gpkg_path, level, cc0)
        if gdf is None:
            print(f"[level {level}] no matching layer found; skipping")
            continue

        # Country filter (works because GID_0 is in every layer).
        if country_codes and "GID_0" in gdf.columns:
            gdf = gdf[gdf["GID_0"].isin(country_codes)]
            if gdf.empty:
                continue

        name_col = _first_present(gdf, spec["name_candidates"])
        if name_col is None:
            print(
                f"[level {level}] no name column in {list(gdf.columns)}; skipping"
            )
            continue

        gid_col = spec["gid_column"]
        if gid_col not in gdf.columns:
            print(f"[level {level}] missing {gid_col}; skipping")
            continue

        varname_col = spec["varname_column"]
        parent_col = f"GID_{level - 1}" if level > 0 else None

        for _, row in gdf.iterrows():
            try:
                centroid = row.geometry.centroid
                lat, lon = float(centroid.y), float(centroid.x)
            except Exception:
                lat, lon = None, None

            parent_code = row[parent_col] if parent_col and parent_col in gdf.columns else None
            synonyms = row[varname_col] if varname_col and varname_col in gdf.columns else ""

            rows.append({
                "concept_code":  row[gid_col],
                "concept_name":  row[name_col],
                "concept_class": spec["concept_class"],
                "parent_code":   parent_code,
                "centroid_lat":  lat,
                "centroid_lon":  lon,
                "synonyms":      synonyms if pd.notna(synonyms) else "",
            })

    df = pd.DataFrame(rows)
    df["vocabulary_id"] = "GADM"
    df["domain"] = "location"
    df["name_normalized"] = df["concept_name"].astype(str).str.lower().str.strip()

    df.to_csv(output_csv, index=False)
    print(f"Wrote {len(df)} location concepts to {output_csv}")
    return df

# ==============================================================
# File: utilities/vocabulary/species.py
# ==============================================================
"""
Build a vector species vocabulary from NCBI Taxonomy.

Correct query strategy:
    1. Look up the genus taxid:      Anopheles[Scientific Name]
    2. Fetch every taxon in the      txid<taxid>[Subtree]
       subtree of that taxid
    3. Batch-efetch records in       Entrez.efetch(..., id=ids)
       chunks of 200

NCBI rate-limits unauthenticated requests to 3 req/s. This script sleeps
0.34 s between requests to stay under the limit. If you set an NCBI API key
via `Entrez.api_key`, you can raise this to 10 req/s (sleep 0.11).
"""

import time
import pandas as pd
from Bio import Entrez


# Curated list of vector genera relevant to VBD surveillance.
VECTOR_TAXA = [
    "Anopheles",
    "Aedes",
    "Culex",
    "Mansonia",
    "Ixodes",
    "Phlebotomus",
]

# NCBI rate limiting.
_REQUEST_INTERVAL = 0.34   # 3 req/s without API key
_FETCH_BATCH = 200         # efetch handles up to a few hundred IDs at once


def _sleep():
    time.sleep(_REQUEST_INTERVAL)


def _get_genus_taxid(genus: str) -> str | None:
    """
    Return the taxid of the *genus* named `genus`, ignoring subgenera,
    series, and other taxa that share the same name.

    NCBI's `[Scientific Name]` search returns every record whose name
    matches, and the esearch result does not include ranks — so we
    esearch for candidates, efetch them, and pick the one whose Rank is
    exactly 'genus'. This prevents the subtree walk from descending into
    a subgenus or a series node by mistake.
    """
    handle = Entrez.esearch(
        db="taxonomy",
        term=f"{genus}[Scientific Name]",
        retmax=20,
    )
    ids = Entrez.read(handle)["IdList"]
    handle.close()
    _sleep()

    if not ids:
        return None

    handle = Entrez.efetch(db="taxonomy", id=ids)
    try:
        records = Entrez.read(handle)
    finally:
        handle.close()
    _sleep()

    target = genus.strip().lower()

    # Preferred: exact name match whose rank is 'genus'.
    for rec in records:
        name = rec.get("ScientificName", "").strip().lower()
        rank = rec.get("Rank", "").strip().lower()
        if name == target and rank == "genus":
            return rec.get("TaxId")

    # Fallback: exact name match at any rank (should not normally fire).
    for rec in records:
        if rec.get("ScientificName", "").strip().lower() == target:
            return rec.get("TaxId")

    return None


def _get_subtree_ids(genus_taxid: str, cap: int = 20000) -> list[str]:
    """
    Return every taxid in the subtree rooted at `genus_taxid`.
    `cap` prevents runaway downloads for very large genera.
    """
    handle = Entrez.esearch(
        db="taxonomy",
        term=f"txid{genus_taxid}[Subtree]",
        retmax=cap,
    )
    ids = Entrez.read(handle)["IdList"]
    handle.close()
    _sleep()
    return ids


def _fetch_taxa(taxon_ids: list[str], genus: str) -> list[dict]:
    """Batch-fetch taxonomy records and normalize into rows."""
    rows = []
    for i in range(0, len(taxon_ids), _FETCH_BATCH):
        batch = taxon_ids[i:i + _FETCH_BATCH]
        handle = Entrez.efetch(db="taxonomy", id=batch)
        try:
            records = Entrez.read(handle)
        finally:
            handle.close()
        _sleep()

        for rec in records:
            # Scientific name + rank + parent
            rank = rec.get("Rank", "")
            name = rec.get("ScientificName", "")
            parent = rec.get("ParentTaxId", "")
            taxid = rec.get("TaxId", "")

            # Synonyms live in OtherNames → Synonym (a list of strings)
            other = rec.get("OtherNames", {})
            syns = other.get("Synonym", []) if isinstance(other, dict) else []
            if isinstance(syns, str):
                syns = [syns]

            rows.append({
                "concept_code":  taxid,
                "concept_name":  name,
                "concept_class": rank,
                "parent_code":   parent,
                "synonyms":      "|".join(syns),
                "genus":         genus,
            })
    return rows


def build_species_vocabulary(
    output_csv: str,
    genera: list | None = None,
    email: str = "you@example.com",
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """
    Fetch species and subspecies under each vector genus from NCBI Taxonomy.

    Parameters
    ----------
    output_csv : str
        Where to write the resulting vocabulary CSV.
    genera : list[str] | None
        Genera to fetch. Defaults to VECTOR_TAXA.
    email : str
        Your email — required by NCBI's terms of use.

    Returns
    -------
    pd.DataFrame
        The vocabulary that was written.
    """
    Entrez.email = email
    genera = genera or VECTOR_TAXA

    all_rows = []
    for genus in genera:
        print(f"[{genus}] looking up taxid ...", flush=True)
        genus_taxid = _get_genus_taxid(genus)
        if not genus_taxid:
            print(f"[{genus}] no taxid found; skipping", flush=True)
            continue

        print(f"[{genus}] taxid={genus_taxid}; fetching subtree ...", flush=True)
        subtree_ids = _get_subtree_ids(genus_taxid)
        print(f"[{genus}] {len(subtree_ids)} taxa in subtree", flush=True)

        # Remove the genus record itself from the fetch so we only get
        # species/subspecies/etc.
        subtree_ids = [i for i in subtree_ids if i != genus_taxid]

        rows = _fetch_taxa(subtree_ids, genus)
        print(f"[{genus}] fetched {len(rows)} records", flush=True)
        all_rows.extend(rows)

    df = pd.DataFrame(all_rows)
    if df.empty:
        print("No species fetched. Check network / Entrez availability.")
        df.to_csv(output_csv, index=False)
        return df

    df["vocabulary_id"] = "NCBI"
    df["domain"] = "vector"
    df["name_normalized"] = df["concept_name"].astype(str).str.lower().str.strip()
    df.to_csv(output_csv, index=False)
    print(f"\nWrote {len(df)} records to {output_csv}")
    return df

End of bundle
